(\mathfrak{p}(2m),q)=1\end{subarray}}\bigg(\prod_{i=1}^{2m}\frac{\lambda_{f}(p_{i})\log p_{i}}{\sqrt{p_{i}}}\widehat{H}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\ell\log p_{i}}{\log Q}}}\right)\bigg).

By Lemma
2.14
, the above equals
\displaystyle\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi_{2m}}R_{1}(\underline{G}),

where

R_{1}(\underline{G}):=\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\nu}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\nu),q)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{H}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\ell\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)^{|G_{j}|}\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in B_{k}(q)}\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}\lambda_{f}(p_{j})^{|G_{j}|}.

By Lemma
2.17
,
R_{1}(\underline{G})
is a linear combination of

(4.6)

\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\nu}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\nu),q)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{H}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\ell\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)^{|G_{j}|}\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in B_{k}(q)}\lambda_{f}(p_{1}^{k_{1}}\cdots p_{\nu}^{k_{\nu}})

over
0\leq k_{j}\leq|G_{j}|
for all
j\leq\nu
.

We apply Lemma
2.2
to the
h
-sum in (
4.6
). If
k_{1}=\cdots=k_{\nu}=0
, then the
h
-sum is bounded by
1
. In this case,
|G_{j}|
must be even by Lemma
2.17
, so the worst case should be
|G_{j}|=2
for all
j\leq\nu=m
. Hence, (
4.6
) is
O((\log Q)^{2m})
when
k_{1}=\cdots=k_{\nu}=0
.
If at least one of the
k_{j}
is nonzero, then (
4.6
) is

\ll\frac{Q^{\epsilon}}{Q}\bigg(\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}\sum_{p_{j}\leq Q^{1/2n\ell}}\frac{\log p_{j}}{p_{j}^{|G_{j}|/2}}p_{j}^{k_{j}/4}\bigg)\ll\frac{Q^{\epsilon}}{Q}\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}Q^{\tfrac{3}{8n\ell}}\ll Q^{-\tfrac{1}{4}+\epsilon}

by Lemma
2.2
and the support of
\widehat{H}
. Thus, we have

R_{1}(\underline{G})\ll Q(\log Q)^{2m},

which implies (
4.5
). This concludes the proof of the proposition.

4.2.
Proof of Proposition
3.2
- set partitions with small contribution

The collection
\Pi_{n}
of all set partitions of
[n]
forms a lattice with the partial ordering given by
\underline{A}\preceq\underline{G}
if every set
G_{i}
in
\underline{G}
is a union of sets in
\underline{A}
.
Then by Lemma
2.14
,
S_{n}(Q;\underline{A})
is a linear combination of

(4.7)

\frac{(-2)^{n}}{(\log Q)^{n}}\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\prod_{G_{j}\in\underline{G}}\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j})

over
\underline{G}\in\Pi_{n}
with
\underline{A}\preceq\underline{G}
, where

(4.8)

\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j}):=\sum_{p\nmid q}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p\lambda_{f}(p)}{\sqrt{p}}}}\right)^{|G_{j}|}H_{G_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p}{\log Q}}}\right).

By Lemma
2.8
for
|G_{j}|=1
and by
|\lambda_{f}(p)|\leq 2
and the prime number theorem for
|G_{j}|\geq 2
, we find that

(4.9)

\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j})\ll\begin{cases}(\log Q)^{2+\epsilon}&\textup{ if }|G_{j}|=1\\
(\log Q)^{2}&\textup{ if }|G_{j}|=2,\\
1&\textup{ if }|G_{j}|>2.\end{cases}

Suppose that
|A_{\ell}|\geq 3
for some
A_{\ell}\in\underline{A}
and
\underline{A}\preceq\underline{G}\in\Pi_{n}
. Then
|G_{\ell}|\geq 3
for some
G_{\ell}\in\underline{G}
. By (
4.9
) for
|G_{j}|\geq 2
and (
4.1
), we find that (
4.7
) is

\ll\frac{1}{(\log Q)^{3}}\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}G_{i}\in\underline{G}\\
G_{i}=\{g_{i}\}\end{subarray}}\frac{|\mathcal{P}_{3}(\{g_{i}\})|}{\log Q}\ll\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{3}}.

This proves (
3.2
).

Next, we need
[
2
, Proposition 4.2]
to prove (
3.3
) and we state it here for the completeness. Note that we changed
\log q
in the proposition to
\log Q
, but the proof remains the same.

Proposition 4.1

(Baluyot, Chandee and Li
[
2
]
)
.

Assume GRH. Let
\Phi
be an even Schwartz function with
\widehat{\Phi}
compactly supported in
(-4,4)
. Then

\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\sum_{p\nmid q}\frac{\lambda_{f}(p)\log p}{\sqrt{p}}\widehat{\Phi}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p}{\log Q}}}\right)\ll Q.

Without loss of generality, we only consider
\underline{A}=\{A_{1},\ldots,A_{\nu}\}\in\Pi_{n}
with
|A_{i}|=2
for all
i\leq\nu-1
and
A_{\nu}=\{1\}
.
If
\underline{A}\preceq\underline{G}
and
\underline{A}\neq\underline{G}
, then
\underline{G}
contains
G_{j}
with
|G_{j}|>2
. By Lemma
2.14
and (
3.2
), we have

S_{n}(Q;\underline{A})=\frac{(-2)^{n}}{(\log Q)^{n}}\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\prod_{A_{j}\in\underline{A}}\mathcal{P}_{3}(A_{j})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{3}}}}\right).

By Lemma
2.17
, we see that
\lambda_{f}(p)^{2}=\lambda_{f}(p^{2})+1
for
p\nmid q
.
By Lemmas
2.9
and
2.11
, we have

(4.10)

\frac{4\mathcal{P}_{3}(A_{j})}{(\log Q)^{2}}=\sum_{p_{j}\nmid q}\frac{4(\log p_{j})^{2}}{(\log Q)^{2}}\frac{(\lambda_{f}(p_{j}^{2})+1)}{p_{j}}H_{A_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)=\mathscr{I}_{2}(A_{j})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log\log Q}{\log Q}}}\right)

for
|A_{j}|=2
.
Hence, we find that

\begin{split}S_{n}(Q;\underline{A})&=\frac{-2}{\log Q}\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{p\nmid q}\frac{\log p\lambda_{f}(p)}{\sqrt{p}}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\\
&\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{j=1}^{\nu-1}\mathscr{I}_{2}(A_{j})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log\log Q}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{3}}}}\right).\end{split}

By Proposition
4.1
and (
4.1
), we obtain (
3.3
).

4.3.
Proof of Proposition
3.3
- Main contribution

We first prove (
3.5
) similarly to the proof of (
3.3
). Let
\underline{A}\in\Pi_{n,2}
. We apply Lemma
2.14
to remove the condition that the primes are distinct, and bound the remaining terms using (
3.2
). Thus we have

S_{n}(Q;\underline{A})=\frac{(-2)^{n}}{(\log Q)^{n}}\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\prod_{A_{j}\in\underline{A}}\mathcal{P}_{3}(A_{j})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{3}}}}\right).

By (
4.10
) and Lemma
2.20
we have

\displaystyle S_{n}(Q;\underline{A})=

\displaystyle\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{A_{j}\in\underline{A}}\mathscr{I}_{2}(A_{j})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log\log Q}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{3}}}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle N_{0}(Q)\prod_{A_{j}\in\underline{A}}\mathscr{I}_{2}(A_{j})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q\log\log Q}{\log Q}}}\right).

By (
1.8
) we have

\sum_{\underline{A}\in\Pi_{n,2}}\frac{S_{n}(Q;\underline{A})}{N_{0}(Q)}=C_{0}(n)+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log\log Q}{\log Q}}}\right).

This proves (
3.5
).

Next, we compute (
3.6
).
Let
K\sqcup K_{0}=[n]
and
K=\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{\kappa}\}
for some
\kappa\geq 2
.
Then by (
3.2
) and Lemma
2.14
, we have

S_{n}(Q;\underline{G}\sqcup\pi_{K,1})=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{A}\in\Pi_{K}\\
|A_{j}|\leq 2\textup{ for all }j\end{subarray}}\mu^{*}(\underline{A})\\
\times\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\prod_{A_{j}\in\underline{A}}\mathcal{P}_{3}(A_{j})\prod_{G_{j}\in\underline{G}}\frac{4\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j})}{(\log Q)^{2}}+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{3}}}}\right)

for
\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K_{0},2}
, where
\pi_{K,1}
is defined in Definition
1
.
By (
4.10
), (
4.1
) and (
4.9
), we find that

S_{n}(Q;\underline{G}\sqcup\pi_{K,1})=\bigg(\prod_{G_{j}\in\underline{G}}\mathscr{I}_{2}(G_{j})\bigg)\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\underline{A}\in\Pi_{K}\\
|A_{j}|\leq 2\textup{ for all }j\end{subarray}}\mu^{*}(\underline{A})\\
\times\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\prod_{A_{j}\in\underline{A}}\mathcal{P}_{3}(A_{j})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q\log\log Q}{\log Q}}}\right).

We once again apply Lemma
2.14
and use the bound in (
4.9
) to convert the sum over
\underline{A}
back into a sum over distinct primes, and obtain that

(4.11)

S_{n}(Q;\underline{G}\sqcup\pi_{K,1})=\bigg(\prod_{G_{j}\in\underline{G}}\mathscr{I}_{2}(G_{j})\bigg)S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q\log\log Q}{\log Q}}}\right).

It remains to compute
S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Assume GRH. Let
K
be a finite set of positive integers with
|K|=\kappa\geq 2
. Then we have

\lim_{Q\to\infty}\frac{S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})}{N_{0}(Q)}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=2\end{subarray}}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime}),

where the function
\mathscr{V}
is in (
1.11
).

We will prove Proposition
4.2
in §
5
. Then it is easy to see that (
1.9
), (
4.11
) and Proposition
4.2
imply
(
3.6
). This concludes the proof of the proposition.

5.
Initial steps toward the proof of Proposition
4.2

Let
K=\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{\kappa}\}
with
\kappa\geq 2
, then we have

S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\sum_{q}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{q}{Q}}}\right)\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),q)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in\mathcal{H}_{k}(q)}\lambda_{f}(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa)).

By Lemma
2.3
, we have

S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d\\
L_{1}|d,\ (L_{2},d)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|L_{1}\\
p^{2}\nmid d\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}}}\right)^{-1}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right)\\
\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),L_{1}L_{2}d)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\sum_{\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}}\frac{\Delta_{d}(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{\ell_{\infty}}.

We first show that only small
L_{1}L_{2}
and
\ell_{\infty}
contribute to the main term.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume GRH. For
|K|=\kappa\geq 2
, we have

S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d\\
L_{1}|d,\ (L_{2},d)=1\\
L_{1}L_{2}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|L_{1}\\
p^{2}\nmid d\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}}}\right)^{-1}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right)\\
\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),L_{1}L_{2}d)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
\ell_{\infty}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Delta_{d}(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{\ell_{\infty}}+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{\log Q}}}\right),

where

\mathcal{L}_{m}:=(\log Q)^{m}.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

After rewriting
\Delta_{d}(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),\ell_{\infty}^{2})
as in (
2.1
),
it suffices to show that

\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d,\ell_{\infty}\\
L_{1}|d,\ (L_{2},d)=1,\ \ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
L_{1}L_{2}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\textup{ or }\ell_{\infty}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|L_{1}\\
p^{2}\nmid d\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2}}}}\right)^{-1}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right)\\
\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),L_{1}L_{2}d)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in B_{k}(d)}\lambda_{f}(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa))\lambda_{f}(\ell_{\infty}^{2})\ll Q(\log Q)^{\kappa-1}.

Since
\lambda_{f}(p_{1}\cdots p_{\kappa})=\lambda_{g}(p_{1}\cdots p_{\kappa})=\lambda_{g}(p_{1})\cdots\lambda_{g}(p_{\kappa})

for some Hecke newform
g
of level dividing
d
and
\lambda_{g}(\ell_{\infty}^{2})\ll\tau(\ell_{\infty}^{2})
, the above sum is

\ll\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d,\ell_{\infty}\\
L_{1}|d,\ (L_{2},d)=1,\ \ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
L_{1}L_{2}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\textup{ or }\ell_{\infty}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau(\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right)\\
\times\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in B_{k}(d)}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \,\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),L_{1}L_{2}d)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\lambda_{g}(p_{j})\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)}}\right|.

By Lemma
2.14
, (
4.8
) and (
4.9
) with
g
and
L_{1}L_{2}d
in place of
f
and
q
, respectively, we find that

\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),L_{1}L_{2}d)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\lambda_{g}(p_{j})\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\ll\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi_{\kappa}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\prod_{G_{j}\in\underline{G}}\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j})}}\right|\ll(\log Q)^{2\kappa+\epsilon}

for any
\epsilon>0
. Moreover, by Lemma
2.2
we have

\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in B_{k}(d)}1=\Delta_{d}(1,1)\ll 1+\frac{\tau(d)}{d^{3/2}}\ll 1.

Hence, it suffices to show that

(5.1)

\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d,\ell_{\infty}\\
L_{1}|d,\ (L_{2},d)=1,\ \ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
L_{1}L_{2}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\textup{ or }\ell_{\infty}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau(\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right)\ll Q(\log Q)^{-\kappa-1-\epsilon_{0}}

for some
\epsilon_{0}>0
.

The sum in (
5.1
) is less than

(5.2)

\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d,\ell_{\infty}\\
L_{1}|d,\ \ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
L_{1}L_{2}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau(\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right)+\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d,\ell_{\infty}\\
L_{1}|d,\ \ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
\ell_{\infty}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau(\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right).

Since

\sum_{\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}}\frac{\tau(\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{\ell_{\infty}}\ll\prod_{p|L_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{3}{p}}}\right)\ll\tau(L_{2}),

the first sum in (
5.2
) is

\displaystyle\ll\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d\\
L_{1}|d,\ L_{1}L_{2}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau(L_{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right)\leq\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}}\sum_{L_{1},L_{2},m}\tau(L_{2})\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}m}{Q}}}\right)\ll\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{\kappa+2}}.

Since

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
\ell_{\infty}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau(\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{\ell_{\infty}}\ll\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
\ell_{\infty}\geq\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\ell_{\infty}^{1-\epsilon}}\leq\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}^{1-2\epsilon}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\ell_{\infty}^{\epsilon}}\ll\frac{\tau(L_{2})}{\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}^{1-2\epsilon}},

the second sum in (
5.2
) is

\displaystyle\ll\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}^{1-2\epsilon}}\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},d\\
L_{1}|d\end{subarray}}\frac{\tau(L_{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}d}{Q}}}\right)\ll\frac{1}{\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}^{1-2\epsilon}}\sum_{L_{1},L_{2},m}\frac{\tau(L_{2})}{L_{1}L_{2}}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}m}{Q}}}\right)\ll\frac{Q}{\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}^{1-2\epsilon}}

for any
\epsilon>0
. This proves (
5.1
) and the lemma follows.
∎

Next, we do changes of variables for the sum in Lemma
5.1
similarly to §6 in
[
2
]
and the proof of Lemma
2.20
.
Since
L_{1}|d
, we let
d=L_{1}m
and apply (
2.23
) to obtain

S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},m\\
(L_{2},m)=1\\
L_{1}L_{2}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})\zeta_{L_{1}}(2)}{L_{1}L_{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1}|(L_{1},m)}\frac{\mu(\ell_{1})}{\ell_{1}^{2}}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}m}{Q}}}\right)\\
\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),L_{1}L_{2}m)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
\ell_{\infty}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Delta_{L_{1}m}(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{\ell_{\infty}}+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{\log Q}}}\right),

where
\zeta_{L_{1}}(2)
is defined in (
2.5
).
We change the condition
\ell_{1}|m
to
m=\ell_{1}n
and then change the condition
(n,L_{2})=1
by putting
\sum_{\ell_{2}|(n,L_{2})}\mu(\ell_{2})
. Then we find that

S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},n\\
L_{1}L_{2}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})\zeta_{L_{1}}(2)}{L_{1}L_{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1}|L_{1}}\frac{\mu(\ell_{1})}{\ell_{1}^{2}}\sum_{\ell_{2}|(n,L_{2})}\mu(\ell_{2})\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}n}{Q}}}\right)\\
\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),L_{1}L_{2}n)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
\ell_{\infty}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\Delta_{L_{1}\ell_{1}n}(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),\ell_{\infty}^{2})}{\ell_{\infty}}+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{\log Q}}}\right).

By removing the condition
\ell_{2}|n
, replacing
n
by
\ell_{2}n
and changing the order of sums, we find that

S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2}\\
L_{1}L_{2}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})\zeta_{L_{1}}(2)}{L_{1}L_{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1}|L_{1}}\frac{\mu(\ell_{1})}{\ell_{1}^{2}}\sum_{\ell_{2}|L_{2}}\mu(\ell_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\\
\ell_{\infty}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\ell_{\infty}}\\
\times\sum_{n}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),L_{1}L_{2}n)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}{Q}}}\right)\Delta_{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),\ell_{\infty}^{2})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{\log Q}}}\right).

We want to remove the condition
(\mathfrak{p}(\kappa),n)=(p_{1}\cdots p_{\kappa},n)=1
in the above sum to apply Kuznetsov’s formula. After relabeling
p_{j}
with
p_{k_{j}}
for
j=1,\ldots,\kappa
, we split the
\#
sum as

\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
(n,\mathfrak{p}(K))=1\end{subarray}}=\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}-\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
(n,\mathfrak{p}(K))\neq 1\end{subarray}}=\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}=K\\
K_{1}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|n\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),n)=1\end{subarray}}.

Hence we obtain the decomposition

(5.3)

S_{\kappa}(Q;\pi_{K,1})=\mathscr{C}_{K}(Q)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}=K\\
K_{1}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2}}(Q)+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{\log Q}}}\right),

where the main term
\mathscr{C}_{K}(Q)
corresponds to the full sum with coprimality condition
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1
, and each
\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2}}(Q)
captures the contribution when
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})
divides
n
but
\mathfrak{p}(K_{2})
does not. More precisely,

(5.4)

\mathscr{C}_{K}(Q):=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{L}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})\zeta_{L_{1}}(2)}{L_{1}L_{2}}\frac{\mu(\ell_{1}\ell_{2})}{\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{\infty}}C_{K}(Q;\mathbb{L}),

where the prime sum is over

(5.5)

\mathbb{L}:=(L_{1},L_{2},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{\infty})

satisfying the conditions

(5.6)

\ell_{1}|L_{1},\penalty\ \ell_{2}|L_{2},\penalty\ L_{1}L_{2}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4},\penalty\ \ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}\penalty\ \textup{ and }\penalty\ \ell_{\infty}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+2}.

\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2}}(Q)
is defined by replacing
C_{K}
to
C_{K_{1},K_{2}}
in (
5.4
),

(5.7)

\begin{split}C_{K}&(Q;\mathbb{L}):=\sum_{n}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}{Q}}}\right)\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\Delta_{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}(\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty}^{2}),\end{split}

and
C_{K_{1},K_{2}}(Q;\mathbb{L})
is defined by adding the conditions
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|n
and
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),n)=1

to the
\#
- sum in (
5.7
). Furthermore, we split
\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2}}(Q)
depending on the contribution of
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})=\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{1}}p_{k_{j}}<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
or
\geq\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}

such that

\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2}}(Q)=\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},<}(Q)+\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q).

Then Proposition
4.2
follows by applying the following lemmas to (
5.3
).

Lemma 5.2
.

Assume GRH.
Let
K
be a set of positive integers such that
|K|\geq 2
. Then we have

\begin{split}\frac{\mathscr{C}_{K}(Q)}{N_{0}(Q)}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=2\end{subarray}}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\log Q)^{-1+\epsilon}}}\right)\end{split}

for any
\epsilon>0
, where the function
\mathscr{V}
is in (
1.11
).

Lemma 5.3
.

Assume GRH. Let
K
be a set of positive integers such that
|K|\geq 2
,
K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}=K
and
K_{1}\neq\emptyset
. Then

\begin{split}\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},<}(Q)\ll\frac{Q}{\log Q}.\end{split}

Lemma 5.4
.

Assume GRH. Let
K
be a set of positive integers such that
|K|\geq 2
,
K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}=K
and
K_{1}\neq\emptyset
. Then

\begin{split}\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q)\ll\frac{Q}{\log Q}.\end{split}

We will prove Lemma
5.2
in §
6
- §
10
and Lemma
5.3
in §
11
by modifying the arguments of the proof of Lemma
5.2
.
We end this section with a proof of Lemma
5.4
.

Proof of Lemma
5.4
.

Without loss of generality and to simplify notation, we only consider the case when
K=[\kappa]
for
\kappa\geq 2
,
K_{1}=[\kappa_{1}]
for
1\leq\kappa_{1}\leq\kappa
and
K=K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}
. Then by the definitions below (
5.4
), we find that

(5.8)

\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q)=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{L}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})\zeta_{L_{1}}(2)}{L_{1}L_{2}}\frac{\mu(\ell_{1}\ell_{2})}{\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{\infty}}C_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q;\mathbb{L}),

where

(5.9)

\begin{split}&C_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q;\mathbb{L}):=\sum_{n}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}{Q}}}\right)\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|n,\penalty\ \mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\geq\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),n)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\Delta_{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}(\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty}^{2}).\end{split}

By replacing
n
with
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n
, we eliminate the condition
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|n
. Then applying the definition of
\Delta_{q}(m,n)
, we have

\displaystyle C_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q;\mathbb{L})=\sum_{n}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),n)=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\geq\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 108.405pt\times\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n}{Q}}}\right)\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in B_{k}(L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n)}\lambda_{f}(\mathfrak{p}(K))\lambda_{f}(\ell_{\infty}^{2}),

where we have taken the
h
-sum over
f
above to be over an Atkin-Lehner basis so that

f=f^{*(g)}=\sum_{\ell|g}\xi_{g}(\ell)f^{*}|_{\ell}

for some newform
f^{*}
of level dividing
L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n
, and some
g|L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n
. By comparing Fourier coefficients we find that

\lambda_{f}(\mathfrak{p}(K))=\sum_{\ell|(g,\mathfrak{p}(K))}\xi_{g}(\ell)\lambda_{f^{*}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{\ell}}}\right).

Since
(g,\mathfrak{p}(K))|(L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n,\mathfrak{p}(K))=\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})
, there is
K_{3}\subset K_{1}
such that
(g,\mathfrak{p}(K))=\mathfrak{p}(K_{3})
. Moreover,
\ell|\mathfrak{p}(K_{3})
is equivalent to
\ell=\mathfrak{p}(K_{4})
for some
K_{4}\subset K_{3}
. Note that
\mathfrak{p}(\emptyset)=1
. Thus, we have

\lambda_{f}(\mathfrak{p}(K))=\lambda_{f^{*}}(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}))\sum_{K_{4}\subset K_{3}}\xi_{g}(\mathfrak{p}(K_{4}))\lambda_{f^{*}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}\setminus K_{4})}}\right).

Hence

\displaystyle C_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q;\mathbb{L})=\sum_{n}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa_{1}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\geq\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{h}}_{f\in B_{k}(L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n)}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n}{Q}}}\right)\lambda_{f}(\ell_{\infty}^{2})\sum_{K_{4}\subset K_{3}}\xi_{g}(\mathfrak{p}(K_{4}))\lambda_{f^{*}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}\setminus K_{4})}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{\kappa_{1}+1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),L_{1}L_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=\kappa_{1}+1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\lambda_{f^{*}}(p_{j})\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right).

The last
\sharp
-sum is by Lemma
2.14

\displaystyle\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{\kappa_{1}+1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),L_{1}L_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=\kappa_{1}+1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\lambda_{f^{*}}(p_{j})\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)=\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K_{2}}}\mu^{*}(\underline{G})\prod_{G_{j}\in\underline{G}}\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j}),

where
\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j})
is defined in (
4.8
) with
q=L_{1}L_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n
. Since
n
can be any positive integer, we have an upper bound for
\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j})
depending on
n,Q
.
By Lemma
2.8
and the prime number theorem, we find that

(5.10)

\mathcal{P}_{3}(G_{j})\ll\begin{cases}(\log Q)^{2+\epsilon}+\log n&\textup{ if }|G_{j}|=1,\\
(\log Q)^{2}&\textup{ if }|G_{j}|=2,\\
1&\textup{ if }|G_{j}|>2.\end{cases}

Hence, we have

\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{\kappa_{1}+1},\ldots,p_{\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),L_{1}L_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=\kappa_{1}+1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\lambda_{f^{*}}(p_{j})\log p_{j}}{\sqrt{p_{j}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\ll(\log Q)^{2\kappa_{2}+\epsilon}+(\log n)^{\kappa_{2}},

where
\kappa_{2}:=|K_{2}|=\kappa-\kappa_{1}
.
Since
\xi_{g}(\ell)\ll\ell^{\epsilon}
by (
2.7
),
\lambda_{f^{*}}(\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}\setminus K_{4}))\ll\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}\setminus K_{4})^{\frac{7}{64}+\epsilon}
and
\lambda_{f}(\ell_{\infty}^{2})\ll\ell_{\infty}^{\frac{7}{32}+\epsilon}
by (
2.3
), we find that

\displaystyle C_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q;\mathbb{L})

\displaystyle\ll\ell_{\infty}^{\frac{7}{32}+\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa_{1}}\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\geq\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})^{\frac{7}{64}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widehat{\Phi}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right|

\displaystyle\times\sum_{n}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n}{Q}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((\log Q)^{2\kappa_{2}+\epsilon}+(\log n)^{\kappa_{2}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\ll Q(\log Q)^{2\kappa_{2}+1+\epsilon}\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{\frac{7}{32}+\epsilon}}{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1},\ldots,p_{\kappa_{1}}\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\geq\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})^{\frac{7}{64}-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\widehat{\Phi}_{j}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{j}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right|

\displaystyle\ll Q(\log Q)^{2\kappa_{2}+1+\epsilon}\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}^{\frac{7}{64}-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{\frac{7}{32}+\epsilon}}{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}.

By applying this bound to
(
5.8
) and the fact that
\kappa-\kappa_{2}=\kappa_{1}\geq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},\geq}(Q)\ll

\displaystyle Q(\log Q)^{2\kappa_{2}-\kappa+1+\epsilon}\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}^{-\frac{25}{64}}\sum_{L_{1},L_{2}}\frac{1}{L_{1}^{3}L_{2}^{2}}\sum_{\ell_{1}|L_{1},\ell_{2}|L_{2}}\frac{1}{\ell_{1}^{3}\ell_{2}}\sum_{\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}}\frac{1}{\ell_{\infty}^{1-\frac{7}{32}-\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle Q(\log Q)^{2\kappa_{2}-\kappa+1+\epsilon}\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}^{-\frac{25}{64}}\ll\frac{Q}{\log Q}.

This proves the lemma.
∎

6.
Applying Kuznetsov’s formula to
\mathscr{C}_{K}(Q)

In this section, we prove Lemma
5.2
. First, we estimate the sum
C_{K}(Q;\mathbb{L})
in (
5.7
) for
\mathbb{L}=(L_{1},L_{2},\ell_{1},\ell_{2},\ell_{\infty})
satisfying (
5.6
). By Petersson’s formula (Lemma
2.1
) we write

\displaystyle C_{K}(Q;\mathbb{L})=2

\displaystyle\pi i^{-k}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\sum_{c\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n\end{subarray}}\frac{S(\ell_{\infty}^{2},\mathfrak{p}(K);cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n)}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}{Q}}}\right)J_{k-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}}}\right).

Next we introduce a smooth partition of unity. Let
V
be a smooth function compactly supported on
[1/2,3]
satisfying
\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{x}{P}}}\right)=1
for all
x\geq 1
, where
\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P}
denotes a sum over
P=2^{j}
for
j\geq 0
. Moreover, let
V_{0}
be a smooth function that is compactly supported in
(\alpha_{1},\beta_{1})
for some
0<\alpha_{1}<1/2
and
\beta_{1}>3
such that
V_{0}(\xi)=1
when
\xi\in[1/2,3]
. By introducing the partition of unity to the prime sums, we find that

(6.1)

C_{K}(Q;\mathbb{L})=2\pi i^{-k}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c\geq 1}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\times\sum_{n}\frac{S(\ell_{\infty}^{2},\mathfrak{p}(K);cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n)}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}J_{k-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}}}\right)H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n},\frac{p_{k_{1}}}{P_{1}},\ldots,\frac{p_{k_{\kappa}}}{P_{\kappa}}}}\right),

where

(6.2)

H(\xi,\bm{\lambda}):=\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{X}{\xi}\sqrt{\lambda_{1}\cdots\lambda_{\kappa}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log(\lambda_{j}P_{j})}{\log Q}}}\right)V_{0}(\lambda_{j})}}\right]

for
\xi\in\mathbb{R}
,
\bm{\lambda}=(\lambda_{1},\ldots,\lambda_{\kappa})\in\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}
and

(6.3)

X:=\frac{4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}}}{cQ}.

Remark 2
.

The dyadic sum over
P_{i}
in (
6.1
) is supported on

(6.4)

P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}\leq Q^{4-\delta}

for some
\delta>0
by the support of the
\widehat{\Phi}_{j}
.

H(\xi,\bm{\lambda})
is nonzero only if
\lambda_{j}\asymp 1
for all
j\leq\kappa
by the support of
V_{0}
and
\alpha_{2}\leq\frac{\xi}{X}\leq\beta_{2}
for some
0<\alpha_{2}<\beta_{2}
by the support of
\Psi
. Let
W
be a smooth function that is compactly supported in
(\alpha_{3},\beta_{3})
for some
0<\alpha_{3}<\alpha_{2}
and
\beta_{3}>\beta_{2}
, and
W(x)=1
for
\alpha_{2}\leq x\leq\beta_{2}
. Then we can multiply
W\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}\frac{1}{X}}}\right)
to the right hand side of (
6.1
) with no harm.

We want to apply the Fourier inversion of
H
. For
u\in\mathbb{R}
and
\bm{v}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{\kappa})\in\mathbb{R}^{\kappa}
, we let

(6.5)

\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}H(\xi,\bm{\lambda})\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\xi u-\lambda_{1}v_{1}-\cdots-\lambda_{\kappa}v_{\kappa}}}\right)d\xi d\lambda_{1}\cdots d\lambda_{\kappa}

be the usual Fourier transform of
H
. For reference later, we record the following bounds on
\widehat{H}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

With notations as above, we have that for any integers
A_{1},A_{2}\geq 0

\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})\ll_{A_{1},A_{2}}\frac{X}{(1+|u|X)^{A_{1}}}\frac{1}{Y(\bm{v})^{A_{2}}},

where
Y(\bm{v})
is defined in (
2.14
).

Proof.

If
\lambda_{j}\asymp 1
for all
j\leq\kappa
and
\xi\asymp X
, then we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial^{n_{0}}}{\partial^{n_{0}}\xi}\frac{\partial^{n_{1}}}{\partial^{n_{1}}\lambda_{1}}\cdots\frac{\partial^{n_{\kappa}}}{\partial^{n_{\kappa}}\lambda_{\kappa}}H(\xi,\bm{\lambda})\ll\frac{1}{X^{n_{0}}}

for any nonnegative integers
n_{0},\ldots,n_{\kappa}
.
The lemma then follows from repeated integration by parts.
∎

Define

(6.6)

\Sigma_{\mathfrak{T}}:=2\pi i^{-k}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c\geq 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})\\
\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}v_{j}}}\right)}}\right)\mathfrak{T}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u)\>du\>dv_{1}\cdots\>dv_{\kappa}.

By the Fourier inversion, (
6.1
) and Remark
2
, we find that

(6.7)

C_{K}(Q;\mathbb{L})=\Sigma_{\mathcal{S}},

where

\mathcal{S}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u):=\sum_{n}\frac{S(\ell_{\infty}^{2},\mathfrak{p}(K);cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n)}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}h_{u}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}}}\right)

and

(6.8)

h_{u}(\xi):=J_{k-1}(\xi)W\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\xi}{X}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(u\xi}}\right).

By Kuznetsov’s formula (Lemma
2.4
) with
N=cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
,
m=\ell_{\infty}^{2}
,
n=\mathfrak{p}(K)
and
\phi=h_{u}
, we find that

(6.9)

\begin{split}\mathcal{S}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u)=\textup{Dis}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u)+\textup{Ctn}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u)+\textup{Hol}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u),\end{split}

where

(6.10)

\begin{split}\textup{Dis}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u)&:=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{\overline{\rho_{j}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2})\rho_{j}(\mathfrak{p}(K))\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)\ell_{\infty}^{2}}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})}h_{u,+}(\kappa_{j}),\\
\textup{Ctn}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u)&:=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{\mathfrak{c}_{\chi}^{2}|M|N}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\rho_{\chi,M,N}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi,M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}h_{u,+}(t)\>dt,\textup{ \;\;\;and}\\
\textup{Hol}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u)&:=\frac{1}{2\pi}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\ell\geq 2\mbox{\scriptsize{ even}}\\
1\leq j\leq\theta_{\ell}(N)\end{subarray}}(\ell-1)!\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)\ell_{\infty}^{2}}\,\overline{\psi_{j,\ell}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2})\psi_{j,\ell}(\mathfrak{p}(K))h_{u,h}(\ell).\end{split}

Here,
h_{u,+}
and
h_{u,h}
are the Bessel transforms of
h_{u}
defined in Lemma
2.4
. Note that the forms appearing in (
6.10
) are of level
cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
.
By (
6.6
), (
6.7
) and (
6.9
), we find that

(6.11)

C_{K}(Q;\mathbb{L})=\Sigma_{\textup{Dis}}+\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}}+\Sigma_{\textup{Hol}}.

Then we have the following propositions.

Proposition 6.2
.

Assume GRH, (
5.6
) and (
6.4
).
For any
\epsilon>0
, we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Dis}}\ll Q^{1-{\delta}/{2}+\epsilon},\qquad\Sigma_{\textup{Hol}}\ll Q^{1-{\delta}/{2}+\epsilon}.

Proposition 6.3
.

Assume GRH, (
5.6
) and (
6.4
). Let
K
be a set of positive integers such that
|K|=\kappa\geq 2
. Then we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}}=\frac{Q(\log Q)^{\kappa}\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{(-2)^{\kappa}L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=2\end{subarray}}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q(\log Q)^{\kappa-1+\epsilon}}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}}}\right)

for any
\epsilon>0
, where the function
\mathscr{V}
is in (
1.11
).

The above propositions imply Lemma
5.2
as follows.

6.1.
Proof of Lemma
5.2

By (
5.4
), (
6.11
) and Propositions
6.2
and
6.3
, we have

\mathscr{C}_{K}(Q)=Q\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}L_{1},L_{2},\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\\
\ell_{1}|L_{1},\ \ell_{2}|L_{2}\\
L_{1}L_{2}<\mathcal{L}_{\kappa+4}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})\zeta_{L_{1}}(2)}{L_{1}^{3}L_{2}^{2}}\frac{\mu(\ell_{1}\ell_{2})}{\ell_{1}^{3}\ell_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=2\end{subarray}}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{1-\epsilon}}}}\right)

for any
\epsilon>0
. By Lemma
2.20
, the sum over
L_{1},L_{2},\ell_{1},\ell_{2}
is asymptotically
T(1)
and we have

\mathscr{C}_{K}(Q)=N_{0}(Q)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=2\end{subarray}}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{1-\epsilon}}}}\right).

This concludes the proof of the lemma.

7.
Proof of Proposition
6.2
- Contribution from Holomorphic forms and Maass forms

In this section we bound
\Sigma_{\textup{Dis}}
only, since bounding the contribution of
\textup{Hol}(c,p_{1}....p_{\kappa})
is similar and easier by the Ramanujan-Petersson bound.
We recall that in the sum
\Sigma_{\textup{Dis}}
,
\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}
denotes a sum over the spectrum of level
cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
, where
\{u_{j}\}_{j=1}^{\infty}
is the orthonormal basis for the Maass forms of level
cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
described in §
2.4
, and
\rho_{j}(n)
denotes the Fourier coefficients of
u_{j}
. In addition, each
u_{j}
is of the form
f^{(g)}
where
f
is a Hecke newform of level
M
with
M|cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
, and
g|\frac{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}{M}
, and
\tau_{f}
is the spectral parameter of
f
, i.e.,
\lambda_{j}=\frac{1}{4}+\kappa_{j}^{2}=\tau_{f}(1-\tau_{f})
.
By (
2.6
)

\rho_{j}(1)=\xi_{g}(1)\rho_{f}(1).

Lemma 7.1
.

Assume GRH, (
5.6
) and (
6.4
). With notation as above, we have

\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\rho_{j}(\mathfrak{p}(K))\prod_{r=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\log p_{k_{r}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{r}}}{P_{r}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{r}\frac{p_{k_{r}}}{P_{r}}}}\right)}}\right)\ll|\rho_{f}(1)|(cQ)^{\epsilon}(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{\epsilon}Y(\bm{v})^{2}

for any
\epsilon>0
, where
Y(\bm{v})
is defined in (
2.14
).

Proof.

Let
V_{0}
be a smooth function that is compactly supported on
(0,\infty)
such that
V_{0}(x)=1
whenever
V(x)\neq 0
. Then we multiply
\prod_{r=1}^{\kappa}V_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{r}}}{P_{r}}}}\right)
to the
\sharp
-sum in the lemma without any changes. By the Mellin inversion we find that

\mathcal{W}_{v_{r}}(x):=\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{r}x}}\right)V(x)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{v_{r}}(it_{r})x^{-it_{r}}dt_{r}

for each
r\leq\kappa
, where
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}
is the Mellin transform of
\mathcal{W}
. Since

\mathcal{W}_{v_{r}}^{(l)}(x)\ll(1+|v_{r}|)^{l}
for every
l\geq 0
and
\mathcal{W}
is compactly supported, we have
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{v_{r}}(it_{r})\ll\frac{(1+|v_{r}|)^{2}}{(1+|t_{r}|)^{2}}
by integration by parts.
Thus, the
\sharp
-sum in the lemma is bounded by

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\ \,\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\rho_{j}(\mathfrak{p}(K))\prod_{r=1}^{\kappa}\frac{\log p_{k_{r}}}{p_{k_{r}}^{it_{r}}}V_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{r}}}{P_{r}}}}\right)}}\right|\prod_{r=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1+|v_{r}|}{1+|t_{r}|}}}\right)^{2}dt_{1}\cdots dt_{\kappa}

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\rho_{f}(1)|(cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}Q)^{\epsilon}(1+|\tau_{f}|)^{\epsilon}Y(\bm{v})^{2}\prod_{r=1}^{\kappa}\frac{\log(2+|t_{r}|)}{(1+|t_{r}|)^{2}}dt_{1}\cdots dt_{\kappa}

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle|\rho_{f}(1)|(cQ)^{\epsilon}(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{\epsilon}Y(\bm{v})^{2},

where the second inequality holds by Lemma

2.16
.

∎

We have
\ell_{\infty}\overline{\rho_{j}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2})\ll(cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2})^{\epsilon}\ell_{\infty}^{1+\epsilon}|\rho_{f}(1)|
by (
2.8
). By this inequality, (
6.6
), (
6.10
) and Lemmas
6.1
and
7.1
, we find that

(7.1)

\begin{split}\Sigma_{\textup{Dis}}\ll Q^{\epsilon}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}c^{\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}\frac{|\rho_{f}(1)|^{2}(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{\epsilon}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})}\frac{X|h_{u,+}(\kappa_{j})|}{(1+|u|X)^{A_{1}}}du\end{split}

for any
A_{1}\geq 0
and
X
defined in (
6.3
). We choose
A_{1}\geq 3
for later uses.
We write

(7.2)

\begin{split}\Sigma_{\textup{Dis}}=\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re}}+\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Im}},\end{split}

where
\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re}}
is the contribution of the real
\kappa_{j}
, and
\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Im}}
is the contribution of the imaginary
\kappa_{j}
corresponding to exceptional eigenvalues.

For
\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Im}}
, we first have

(7.3)

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{X|h_{u,+}(\kappa_{j})|}{(1+|u|X)^{A_{1}}}du\ll\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}}}\right)\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{X^{k-1},\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}}}\right\}\ll\frac{\min\{1,X^{k-1}\}}{\sqrt{X}}

by Lemma
2.6
(2).
Next we need the spectral large sieve bound

(7.4)

\sum_{|\kappa_{j}|\leq x}\frac{|\rho_{j}(1)|^{2}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})}\ll x^{2}

from Deshoulliers and Iwaniec
[
14
, Theorem 2]
. Hence, we find that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Im}}\ll

\displaystyle Q^{\epsilon}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}c^{\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa_{j}=ir\\
|r|<1/2\end{subarray}}\frac{|\rho_{f}(1)|^{2}(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{\epsilon}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})}\frac{\min\{1,X^{k-1}\}}{\sqrt{X}}

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle Q^{\epsilon}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}c^{\epsilon}\frac{\min\{1,X^{k-1}\}}{\sqrt{X}}

by (
7.1
) - (
7.4
). By (
5.6
), (
6.3
) and (
6.4
), we have

\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}c^{\epsilon}\frac{\min\{1,X^{k-1}\}}{\sqrt{X}}\ll\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}}}{Q}}}\right)^{1+\epsilon}\ll Q^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\epsilon}.

Hence, for any
\epsilon>0
, we have

(7.5)

\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Im}}\ll Q^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\epsilon}.

For
\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re}}
,
by (
7.1
), (
7.2
) and Lemma
2.6
(1), we find that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re}}\ll

\displaystyle Q^{\epsilon}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}c^{\epsilon}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sum_{\kappa_{j}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\rho_{f}(1)|^{2}(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{\epsilon}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})}

\displaystyle\times\frac{1}{F^{1-\epsilon}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{F}{1+|\kappa_{j}|}}}\right)^{C(j)}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{X^{k-1},\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}}}\right\}\frac{X(1+|\log X|)}{(1+|u|X)^{A_{1}}}du

for some
F<(|u|+1)(1+X)
and for any choice of
C(j)\geq 0
. Since
F
depends on
u
and
X
, we first estimate the
j
-sum

\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re},1}:=\sum_{\kappa_{j}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{|\rho_{f}(1)|^{2}(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{\epsilon}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})F^{1-\epsilon}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{F}{1+|\kappa_{j}|}}}\right)^{C(j)}.

Each newform
f
appears at most
\ll(cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2})^{\epsilon}
times in the above
j
-sum as
u_{j}=f^{(g)}
with
g|\frac{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}{M}
. When
g=1
, we have
u_{j}=f^{(1)}=f
and so
\rho_{j}(1)=\rho_{f}(1)
.
Then we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re},1}\ll(cQ)^{\epsilon}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\kappa_{j}\in\mathbb{R}\\
u_{j}=f^{(1)}\textup{ for some }f\end{subarray}}\frac{|\rho_{j}(1)|^{2}(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{\epsilon}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})F^{1-\epsilon}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{F}{1+|\kappa_{j}|}}}\right)^{C(j)}.

We can change the above sum to the sum over all real
\kappa_{j}
by adding more positive terms. By splitting the sum dyadically and applying (
7.4
), we find that

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re},1}

\displaystyle\ll(cQ)^{\epsilon}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{|\kappa_{j}|\leq F}\frac{|\rho_{j}(1)|^{2}(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{\epsilon}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})F^{1-\epsilon}}+\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{\ell}\sum_{\ell F<|\kappa_{j}|\leq 2\ell F}\frac{|\rho_{j}(1)|^{2}}{\cosh(\pi\kappa_{j})}\frac{F^{2+2\epsilon}}{(1+|\kappa_{j}|)^{3}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\ll(cQ)^{\epsilon}F^{1+\epsilon}\ll(cQ)^{\epsilon}(1+|u|)^{1+\epsilon}(1+X)^{1+\epsilon}

for any
\epsilon>0
, where we have chosen
C(j)=0
for
|\kappa_{j}|\leq F
and
C(j)=3+\epsilon
otherwise.

Since

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}(1+|u|)^{1+\epsilon}\frac{X}{(1+|u|X)^{A_{1}}}du\ll\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{X}}}\right)^{1+\epsilon}

for
A_{1}\geq 3
,
we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re}}\ll

\displaystyle Q^{\epsilon}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}c^{\epsilon}\frac{(1+X)^{2+2\epsilon}}{X^{1+\epsilon}}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{X^{k-1},\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}}}\right\}(1+|\log X|).

Since
k\geq 4
, the
c
-sum is convergent and bounded by

\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}}}{Q}}}\right)^{1+\epsilon}
for any
\epsilon>0
. This may be verified by dividing the sum into two depending on
c\leq\frac{4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}}}{Q}
or not. By (
6.4
), we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Re}}\ll

\displaystyle\ell_{\infty}^{1+\epsilon}(L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}Q)^{\epsilon}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}}}{Q}}}\right)^{1+\epsilon}\ll Q^{1-\frac{\delta}{2}+\epsilon},

which has the same bound as
\Sigma_{\textup{Dis},\textup{Im}}
in (
7.5
). Thus,
\Sigma_{\textup{Dis}}
has the same bound as well, which proves the first inequality of the proposition. As we mentioned in the beginning of this section, we omit the proof of the holomorphic case, since it is similar and easier.

8.
Proof of Proposition
6.3
- Contribution from Eisenstein series

Recall that
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}}
is defined in (
6.6
) and (
6.10
).
Let
\textup{Ctn}_{0}
and
\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}
be the contribution of the trivial character
\chi_{0}
and the nontrivial characters, respectively, in (
6.10
). Then we have

(8.1)

\textup{Ctn}_{0}(c,\mathfrak{p}(K);u):=\frac{1}{4\pi}\sum_{M|N}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\rho_{\chi_{0},M,N}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}h_{u,+}(t)\>dt

with
N=cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
and
\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}
is the same as
Ctn
defined in (
6.10
) except for
\mathfrak{c}_{\chi}\neq 1
. Since
\textup{Ctn}=\textup{Ctn}_{0}+\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}
by definition, we see that

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}}=\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}+\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}}

by (
6.6
). Then it is easy to see that Proposition
6.3
follows from the two propositions below.

Proposition 8.1
.

Assume GRH, (
5.6
) and (
6.4
).
With notation as above and for any
\epsilon>0
, we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}}\ll Q^{\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{\delta}{4}+\epsilon}.

Proposition 8.2
.

Assume RH, (
5.6
) and (
6.4
). Let
K
be a set of positive integers such that
|K|=\kappa\geq 2
. With notation as above and for any
\epsilon>0
, we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\frac{Q(\log Q)^{\kappa}\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{(-2)^{\kappa}L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=2\end{subarray}}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime})+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q(\log Q)^{\kappa-1+\epsilon}}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}}}\right),

where the function
\mathscr{V}
is in (
1.11
).

We will prove Proposition
8.1
in §
8.1
and Proposition
8.2
in §
9
and §
10
.

8.1.
Proof of Proposition
8.1
: bounding the contributions of the non-trivial character

We begin by proving the following lemma.

Lemma 8.3
.

Suppose that
\chi\bmod c_{\chi}
is non-trivial such that
c_{\chi}^{2}|M|N
and
N=cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
. Under the same assumptions as in Proposition
8.1
, we have

(8.2)

\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},...,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{j}\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)}}\right)\rho_{\chi,M,N}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi,M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}\\
\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(NQ(1+|t|))^{\epsilon}Y(\bm{v})^{3}.

Proof.

By (
2.4
) and the fact that
|\widetilde{C}(\chi,M,t)|=1
, we find that

\displaystyle\rho_{\chi,M,N}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi,M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}

\displaystyle=\frac{M_{1}\zeta_{(M,\frac{N}{M})}(1)\ell_{\infty}^{-2it}\overline{\rho^{\prime}_{\chi,M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}}{M_{2}N|L^{(N)}(1+2it,\chi^{2})|^{2}}\sum_{m_{2}|M_{2}}m_{2}\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M_{2}}{m_{2}}}}\right)\bar{\chi}(m_{2})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}n_{1}n_{2}=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{M_{1}m_{2}}\\
(n_{2},N/M)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)^{it}\chi(n_{2})\bar{\chi}(n_{1})}{n_{2}^{2it}}

where

M=\mathfrak{c}_{\chi}M_{1}M_{2}
,
(M_{2},\mathfrak{c}_{\chi})=1
and
M_{1}|\mathfrak{c}_{\chi}^{\infty}
. Note that

\rho^{\prime}_{\chi,M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)\ll\ell_{\infty}^{2+\epsilon}N^{\epsilon}
and
\zeta_{(M,N/M)}(1)\leq\tau(N)\ll N^{\epsilon}
for any
\epsilon>0
. We also need a well-known bound

\frac{1}{L^{(N)}(1+2it,\chi^{2})}=\frac{1}{L(1+2it,\chi^{2}\chi_{N,0})}\ll N^{\epsilon}(1+|t|)^{\epsilon}

for any
\epsilon>0
, where
\chi_{N,0}
is the principal character modulo
N
. See §11 of
[
34
]
for a proof. Hence, the
\sharp
-sum in (
8.2
) is

(8.3)

\ll\frac{M_{1}\ell_{\infty}^{2+\epsilon}(1+|t|)^{\epsilon}}{M_{2}N^{1-\epsilon}}\sum_{m_{2}|M_{2}}m_{2}\bigg|\penalty\ \penalty\ \sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},...,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K)=n_{1}n_{2}M_{1}m_{2}\\
(n_{2},N/M)=1\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{p_{k_{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}-it}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{j}\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)}}\right)\frac{\bar{\chi}(n_{1})\chi(n_{2})}{n_{2}^{2it}}\bigg|.

Since
\mathfrak{p}(K)=n_{1}n_{2}M_{1}m_{2}
and the
p_{k_{j}}
are distinct primes, we write
n_{1}=\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})
,
n_{2}=\mathfrak{p}(K_{2})
,
M_{1}=\mathfrak{p}(K_{3})
and
m_{2}=\mathfrak{p}(K_{4})
for
K_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup K_{4}=K
.
By Lemma
2.15
, the
\sharp
-sum in (
8.3
) is

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup K_{4}=K}\bigg|\penalty\ \penalty\ \sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},...,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),N/M)=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{3})=M_{1},\mathfrak{p}(K_{4})=m_{2}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{p_{k_{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}-it}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{j}\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\frac{\chi(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}))\bar{\chi}(\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}))}{\mathfrak{p}(K_{2})^{2it}}\bigg|

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup K_{4}=K}(M_{1}m_{2})^{-\frac{1}{2}+\epsilon}\bigg|\penalty\ \penalty\ \sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k}\textup{ for }k\in K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}),L_{1}L_{2}M_{1}m_{2})=1\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),L_{1}L_{2}M_{1}m_{2}N/M)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j\in K_{1}}\frac{\bar{\chi}(p_{k_{j}})\log p_{k_{j}}}{p_{k_{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}-it}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{j}\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\prod_{j\in K_{2}}\frac{\chi(p_{k_{j}})\log p_{k_{j}}}{p_{k_{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}+it}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(v_{j}\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\bigg|

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{M_{1}m_{2}}}(NQ(1+|t|))^{\epsilon}Y(\bm{v})^{3}

for any
\epsilon>0
.
Therefore, by combining the above inequalities, the
\sharp
-sum in (
8.2
) is

\ll\frac{M_{1}}{M_{2}N}\sum_{m_{2}|M_{2}}m_{2}\frac{1}{\sqrt{M_{1}m_{2}}}(NQ(1+|t|))^{\epsilon}Y(\bm{v})^{3}\ll\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(NQ(1+|t|))^{\epsilon}Y(\bm{v})^{3}.

∎

By (
6.6
) and Lemma
8.3
, we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}}\ll\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}|\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})|\\
\times\sum_{1\neq\mathfrak{c}_{\chi}^{2}|M|N}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(NQ(1+|t|))^{\epsilon}Y(\bm{v})^{3}|h_{u,+}(t)|dtdudv_{1}\cdots dv_{\kappa}

for
N=cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}
.
By Lemma
6.1
and Lemma
2.6
(1), we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}}\ll\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{X^{k-1},\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}}}\right\}\\
\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{X}{(1+|u|X)^{A}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N}}(NQ(1+|t|))^{\epsilon}\frac{1+|\log X|}{F^{1-\epsilon}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{F}{1+|t|}}}\right)^{C}dtdu

for some
F<(|u|+1)(1+X)
and
A,C\geq 0
and for any
\epsilon>0
, where
X
is defined in (
6.3
). By choosing
A=2
and
C=1+2\epsilon
, we see that

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}}\ll Q^{\epsilon}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c}\frac{1}{c^{1/2-\epsilon}}\min\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left\{X^{k-1},\frac{1}{\sqrt{X}}}}\right\}(1+|\log X|)(1+X)^{3\epsilon}.

The
c
-sum is bounded by
\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}}}{Q}}}\right)^{1/2+\epsilon}
, which may be verified by dividing the sum into two depending on
c\leq\frac{4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}}}{Q}
. Since the
d
-sum is supported on
P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}\ll Q^{4-\delta}
, we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{\textup{non}}}\ll Q^{\epsilon}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{P_{1}\cdots P_{\kappa}}}{Q}}}\right)^{1/2+\epsilon}\ll Q^{\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{\delta}{4}+\epsilon}.

9.
Contribution from the trivial character – Off-diagonal main terms

In this section, we start to compute
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}
defined in (
6.6
) and (
8.1
) assuming GRH and (
5.6
) for
\mathbb{L}
. By Lemmas
2.6
and
6.1
, we can change the order of the sums and the integrals, so that

(9.1)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\frac{i^{-k}}{2}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\times\sum_{c\geq 1}\sum_{M|cL_{0}}\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}\\
\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h_{u,+}(t)\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}v_{j}}}\right)dv_{1}\cdots\>dv_{\kappa}dudt,

where

(9.2)

L_{0}:=L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}.

Next, we apply the Fourier inversion to the
\bm{v}
-integrals and the
u
-integral. Let

\widehat{H_{\xi}}(\bm{v}):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}H(\xi,\bm{\lambda})\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\bm{v}\cdot\bm{\lambda}}}\right)d\lambda_{1}\cdots d\lambda_{\kappa},

then we see that
\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{H_{\xi}}(\bm{v})\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-u\xi}}\right)d\xi
. By the Fourier inversion, we have

H(\xi,\bm{\lambda})=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{H_{\xi}}(\bm{v})\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\bm{v}\cdot\bm{\lambda}}}\right)dv_{1}\cdots dv_{\kappa}
. By combining the above and by (
6.2
) and (
6.3
), we find that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}v_{j}}}\right)dv_{1}\cdots dv_{\kappa}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}H\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\xi,\frac{p_{k_{1}}}{P_{1}},\ldots,\frac{p_{k_{\kappa}}}{P_{\kappa}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-u\xi}}\right)d\xi

(9.3)

\displaystyle=\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left[\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)V_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)}}\right]\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{\xi cQ}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-u\xi}}\right)d\xi.

By (
6.8
) and Lemma
2.4
we see that

h_{u,+}(t)=\frac{2\pi i}{\sinh(\pi t)}\int_{0}^{\infty}(J_{2it}(\xi)-J_{-2it}(\xi))J_{k-1}(\xi)W\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\xi}{X}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(u\xi}}\right)\frac{d\xi}{\xi}.

Since
W
is compactly supported, the above integral can be extended to the integral over
\mathbb{R}
. Hence, by the Fourier inversion, we have

(9.4)

\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}h_{u,+}(t)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-u\xi}}\right)du=\frac{2\pi i}{\sinh(\pi t)}(J_{2it}(\xi)-J_{-2it}(\xi))J_{k-1}(\xi)W\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\xi}{X}}}\right)\frac{1}{\xi}.

Hence, by (
9.1
) - (
9.4
), we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\frac{i^{-k}}{2}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\times\sum_{c\geq 1}\sum_{M|cL_{0}}\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}\\
\times\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{\xi cQ}}}\right)(J_{2it}(\xi)-J_{-2it}(\xi))J_{k-1}(\xi)W\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\xi}{X}}}\right)\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\frac{2\pi idt}{\sinh(\pi t)}.

Here, the factor
V_{0}
has been removed using its definition in the beginning of §6.

By the definition of
W
in Remark
2
, we can change that the
\xi
-integral is over
[0,\infty)
and remove
W(\xi/X)
. We can also remove the
d
-sum and the factors
V(p_{k_{j}}/P_{j})
by the fact that
\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P}V(x/P)=1
for
x\geq 1
. Hence, we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\frac{i^{-k}}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\times\sum_{c\geq 1}\sum_{M|cL_{0}}\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}\\
\times\int_{0}^{\infty}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{\xi cQ}}}\right)(J_{2it}(\xi)-J_{-2it}(\xi))J_{k-1}(\xi)\frac{d\xi}{\xi}\frac{2\pi idt}{\sinh(\pi t)}.

By the Mellin inversion (
2.24
) and changing the order of sums and integrals, which may be justified by Lemma
2.18
, we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\frac{i^{-k}}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{(-\epsilon_{1})}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(s)Q^{s}}{(4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{s}}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{p_{k_{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}(1+s)}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)

(9.5)

\displaystyle\times\widetilde{\varrho}_{L_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(-s)(J_{2it}(\xi)-J_{-2it}(\xi))J_{k-1}(\xi)\xi^{s-1}dsd\xi\frac{dt}{\sinh(\pi t)}

for
0<\epsilon_{1}<k-1
, where

(9.6)

\widetilde{\varrho}_{L_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s):=\sum_{c\geq 1}\frac{1}{c^{s}}\sum_{M|cL_{0}}\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}.

9.1.
Fourier coefficients of Eisenstein series

We want to show that
\widetilde{\varrho}_{L_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s)
in (
9.6
) has an analytic continuation.
It requires the following lemma.

Lemma 9.1
.

Let
L_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty}
be as above, then we have

\widetilde{\varrho}_{L_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s)=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)^{it}}{L_{0}\ell_{\infty}^{2it}|\zeta(1+2it)|^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)\\
d_{2}|\ell_{\infty}^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d_{1}d_{2})}{d_{1}^{2it}d_{2}^{-2it}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)/d_{1}\\
c_{2}|\ell_{\infty}^{2}/d_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{c_{2}^{2it}}{c_{1}^{2it}}F_{L_{0},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)

for
\textup{Re}(s)>0
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathfrak{m}=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{c_{1}c_{2}d_{1}d_{2}}
, where

(9.7)

F_{\alpha,r,\mathfrak{m}}(s,it):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c\\
r|c\alpha\end{subarray}}\frac{|\zeta_{c\alpha}(1+2it)|^{2}}{c^{1+s}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\mid c\alpha/r\\
p^{2}|c\alpha\end{subarray}}\bigg(\frac{p\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}}{p-1}\bigg)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\mid c\alpha/r\\
p^{2}\nmid c\alpha\end{subarray}}\bigg(\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}+\frac{1}{p}\bigg).

Proof.

Since
\mathfrak{c}_{\chi}=1
,
M_{1}=1
and
M_{2}=M
in (
2.4
) for
\chi=\chi_{0}
, we have

(9.8)

\rho_{\chi_{0},M,N}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}\\
=\frac{\zeta_{(M,N/M)}(1)|\zeta_{N}(1+2it)|^{2}}{MN|\zeta(1+2it)|^{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)^{it}}{\ell_{\infty}^{2it}}\rho^{\prime}_{\chi_{0},M,N}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho^{\prime}_{\chi_{0},M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}

and

\displaystyle\rho^{\prime}_{\chi_{0},M,N}(n,t)=

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1}|M}m_{1}\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{1}}}}\right)\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{0}|n/m_{1}\\
(c_{0},N/M)=1\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{c_{0}^{2it}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{m_{1}|M}m_{1}\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{1}}}}\right)\sum_{c_{0}|n/m_{1}}\frac{1}{c_{0}^{2it}}\sum_{d_{1}|(c_{0},N/M)}\mu(d_{1}).

We replace the condition
d_{1}|c_{0}
by a substitution
c_{1}=c_{0}/d_{1}
. After changing the order of the sums, we find that

(9.9)

\rho^{\prime}_{\chi_{0},M,N}(n,t)=\sum_{d_{1}|(n,N/M)}\frac{\mu(d_{1})}{d_{1}^{2it}}\sum_{c_{1}|n/d_{1}}\frac{1}{c_{1}^{2it}}\sum_{m_{1}|(M,n/d_{1}c_{1})}m_{1}\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{1}}}}\right).

By (
9.8
) and (
9.9
), we have

\rho_{\chi_{0},M,N}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,N}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}=\frac{\zeta_{(M,N/M)}(1)|\zeta_{N}(1+2it)|^{2}}{MN|\zeta(1+2it)|^{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)^{it}}{\ell_{\infty}^{2it}}\\
\times\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{1}|(\mathfrak{p}(K),N/M)\\
d_{2}|(\ell_{\infty}^{2},N/M)\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d_{1})}{d_{1}^{2it}}\frac{\mu(d_{2})}{d_{2}^{-2it}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)/d_{1}\\
c_{2}|\ell_{\infty}^{2}/d_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{c_{2}^{2it}}{c_{1}^{2it}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}|(M,\mathfrak{p}(K)/d_{1}c_{1})\\
m_{2}|(M,\ell_{\infty}^{2}/d_{2}c_{2})\end{subarray}}m_{1}m_{2}\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{1}}}}\right)\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{2}}}}\right).

Since
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{2})=1
and
\ell_{\infty}|L_{2}^{\infty}
, we have
(\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty}^{2})=(m_{1},m_{2})=(d_{1},d_{2})=1
. Then at most one of
m_{1}
and
m_{2}
is divisible by
p
for each prime
p|M
. If
M
is not squarefree, then
\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{1}}}}\right)\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{2}}}}\right)=0
. For a squarefree
M
, we have

\sum_{m_{1}|(M,\mathfrak{m}_{1})}m_{1}\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{1}}}}\right)=\mu(M)\prod_{p|(M,\mathfrak{m}_{1})}(1-p),

so that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}m_{1}|(M,\mathfrak{p}(K)/d_{1}c_{1})\\
m_{2}|(M,\ell_{\infty}^{2}/d_{2}c_{2})\end{subarray}}m_{1}m_{2}\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{1}}}}\right)\mu\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{M}{m_{2}}}}\right)=\mu(M)^{2}\prod_{p|(M,\mathfrak{m})}(1-p)

for
\mathfrak{m}=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{c_{1}c_{2}d_{1}d_{2}}
.
By letting
N=cL_{0}
and changing the order of the sums, we find that

(9.10)

\sum_{M|cL_{0}}\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL_{0}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}\\
=\frac{|\zeta_{cL_{0}}(1+2it)|^{2}}{cL_{0}|\zeta(1+2it)|^{2}}\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)^{it}}{\ell_{\infty}^{2it}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{1}|(\mathfrak{p}(K),cL_{0})\\
d_{2}|(\ell_{\infty}^{2},cL_{0})\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d_{1}d_{2})}{d_{1}^{2it}d_{2}^{-2it}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)/d_{1}\\
c_{2}|\ell_{\infty}^{2}/d_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{c_{2}^{2it}}{c_{1}^{2it}}g\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(cL_{0};d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}}\right),

where

g(cL_{0};d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}):=\sum_{M|cL_{0}/d_{1}d_{2}}\frac{\mu(M)^{2}}{M}\zeta_{(M,cL_{0}/M)}(1)\prod_{p|(M,\mathfrak{m})}(1-p).

By multiplicativity, we find the product formula

\displaystyle g(cL_{0};d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m})=

\displaystyle\prod_{p|cL_{0}/d_{1}d_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1-p\delta_{p|\mathfrak{m}}}{p}\zeta_{(p,cL_{0}/p)}(1)}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\mid cL_{0}/d_{1}d_{2}\\
p^{2}|cL_{0}\end{subarray}}\bigg(\frac{p\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}}{p-1}\bigg)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\mid cL_{0}/d_{1}d_{2}\\
p^{2}\nmid cL_{0}\end{subarray}}\bigg(\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}+\frac{1}{p}\bigg).

One can easily complete the proof of the lemma by multiplying
c^{-s}
to (
9.10
), summing it over
c
and then changing the order of sums.
∎

Lemma
9.1
says that
\widetilde{\varrho}_{L_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s)
is a combination of finitely many
F_{L_{0},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)
. Hence, to find an analytic continuation of
\widetilde{\varrho}_{L_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s)
, it is enough to observe
F_{\alpha,r,\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)
.
Here is a product formula for
F_{\alpha,r,\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)
with some conditions applicable to
F_{L_{0},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)
.

Lemma 9.2
.

Let
r,\alpha,\mathfrak{m}\in\mathbb{N}
,
t\in\mathbb{R}
,
\beta=(r,\alpha)
,
r=r_{1}\beta
and
\alpha=\alpha_{1}\beta
. Assume that
r
is squarefree with
(r,\alpha_{1})=1
and that every prime
p|\mathfrak{m}
satisfies
p^{2}\nmid\alpha_{1}
. Let
F_{\alpha,r,\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)
be defined in (
9.7
), then we have

(9.11)

\begin{split}F_{\alpha,r,\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=&\zeta(1+s)\widetilde{F}(s,it)\frac{1}{r_{1}^{1+s}}\prod_{p|r}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}}{p^{s}(p-1)}}}\right)\\
&\times\prod_{p||\alpha_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}+\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{s+1}(p-1)}}}\right)}}\right)\prod_{p^{2}|\alpha_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p}{p-1}}}\right)\\
&\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{m}\\
p\nmid r\alpha_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1},\end{split}

where

W_{p}(s,z)=1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2z}}-\frac{1}{p^{1-2z}}+\frac{1}{p^{2+2z+s}}+\frac{1}{p^{2-2z+s}}+\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{3+s}}+\frac{1}{p^{3+2s}(p-1)}+\frac{1}{p^{2}}

and

\widetilde{F}(s,z)=\prod_{p}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2z}}}}\right)^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-2z}}}}\right)^{-1}W_{p}(s,z)}}\right)

for
\textup{Re}(s)>-\frac{1}{2}
and
-\frac{1}{4}<\textup{Re}(z)<\frac{1}{4}
. Moreover, define

(9.12)

\begin{split}\widetilde{F}_{0}(s,z)&:=\prod_{p}\bigg(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2z}}}}\right)^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-2z}}}}\right)^{-1}\\
&\hskip 72.26999pt\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2+2z+s}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2-2z+s}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}}}\right)W_{p}(s,z)\bigg),\end{split}

then it is convergent when
\textup{Re}(\pm 2z+s)>-\frac{3}{2}
and
|\textup{Re}(z)|<\frac{1}{4}
, and bounded when
\textup{Re}(\pm 2z+s)\geq-\frac{3}{2}+\epsilon
and
|\textup{Re}(z)|\leq\frac{1}{4}-\epsilon
for every
\epsilon>0
. It also satisfies

\widetilde{F}(s,z)=\zeta(2+s)\zeta(2+2z+s)\zeta(2-2z+s)\widetilde{F}_{0}(s,z).

Proof.

Since
(r,\alpha)=\beta
, we have
r_{1}\alpha=r\alpha_{1}=r_{1}\alpha_{1}\beta
. The condition
r|\alpha c
in the definition of
F(s)
is equivalent to
r_{1}|c
. Then we have

F_{\alpha,r,\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=\sum_{c}\frac{|\zeta_{r\alpha_{1}}(1+2it)|^{2}}{r_{1}^{1+s}c^{1+s}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|c\\
(p,r\alpha_{1})=1\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2it}}}}\right|^{-2}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\alpha_{1}c\\
p^{2}|r\alpha_{1}c\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}}{p-1}}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\alpha_{1}c\\
p^{2}\nmid r\alpha_{1}c\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}+\frac{1}{p}}}\right).

Since
r
is squarefree with
(r,\alpha_{1})=1
, we treat four types of primes differently according to
p^{2}|\alpha_{1}
,
p||\alpha_{1}
,
p|r
and
p\nmid r\alpha_{1}
. In case of
p^{2}|\alpha_{1}
,
\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}=1
by an assumption of the lemma. Thus, by multiplicativity, we find that

\displaystyle F_{\alpha,r,\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=

\displaystyle\frac{|\zeta_{r\alpha_{1}}(1+2it)|^{2}}{r_{1}^{1+s}}\prod_{p|r}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{p}{p-1}\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\frac{1}{p^{\ell(1+s)}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p||\alpha_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{p}{p-1}\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}\sum_{\ell\geq 1}\frac{1}{p^{\ell(1+s)}}}}\right)\prod_{p^{2}|\alpha_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p}{p-1}\sum_{\ell\geq 0}\frac{1}{p^{\ell(1+s)}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p\nmid r\alpha_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}+\frac{1}{p}}}\right)\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{p}{p-1}\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}\sum_{\ell\geq 2}\frac{1}{p^{\ell(1+s)}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2it}}}}\right|^{-2}}}\right).

By multiplying
1=\zeta(1+s)\prod_{p}(1-p^{-1-s})
and dividing two cases in the last product depending on
p|\mathfrak{m}
, we find that

(9.13)

\begin{split}F_{\alpha,r,\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=&\frac{|\zeta_{r\alpha_{1}}(1+2it)|^{2}}{r_{1}^{1+s}}\zeta(1+s)\prod_{p|r}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}}{p^{s}(p-1)}}}\right)\\
&\times\prod_{p||\alpha_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}+\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{s+1}(p-1)}}}\right)}}\right)\prod_{p^{2}|\alpha_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p}{p-1}}}\right)\\
&\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{m}\\
p\nmid r\alpha_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2it}}}}\right|^{-2}}}\right)}}\right)\widetilde{F}_{r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}(s,it),\end{split}

where

\widetilde{F}_{r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}(s,z):=\prod_{p\nmid r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}\bigg(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p}}}\right)\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{p}{p-1}\sum_{\ell\geq 2}\frac{1}{p^{\ell(1+s)}}}}\right)}{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2z}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-2z}}}}\right)}\bigg).

It is easy to check that

\widetilde{F}_{r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}(s,z)=\prod_{p\nmid r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2z}}}}\right)^{-1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1-2z}}}}\right)^{-1}W_{p}(s,z)}}\right).

Since
\widetilde{F}(s,z)
is the same product as
\widetilde{F}_{r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}(s,z)
except for the primes
p|r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}
, we see that

|\zeta_{r\alpha_{1}}(1+2it)|^{2}\widetilde{F}_{r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=\widetilde{F}(s,it)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{m}\\
p\nmid r\alpha_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|1-\frac{1}{p^{1+2it}}}}\right|^{2}\prod_{p|r\alpha_{1}\mathfrak{m}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}.

By applying the above equation to (
9.13
), we obtain (
9.11
). It is easy to show the remaining part of the lemma, so we omit the proof.
∎

Remark 3
.

Due to the factor
\mu(L_{1}L_{2})
and the condition
\ell_{1}|L_{1}
and
\ell_{2}|L_{2}
in (
5.4
),
L_{1},L_{2},\ell_{1},\ell_{2}
are squarefree and
(L_{1}\ell_{1},\ell_{2})=1
.
Let
\beta=(d_{1}d_{2},L_{0})
,
d_{1}d_{2}=\beta k_{1}
and
L_{0}=\beta\alpha_{1}
. Since
(d_{1},L_{1}L_{2})=1
,
\beta=(d_{2},L_{0})=(d_{2},\ell_{2})
, and
\beta|\ell_{2}
. Thus
(\alpha_{1},\beta)=(\ell_{2}/\beta,\beta)=1
and
(\alpha_{1},d_{1}d_{2})=(L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}/\beta,d_{2})=(\ell_{2}/\beta,d_{2})=1.
Moreover, every prime
p|\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}d_{1}}\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{c_{2}d_{2}}
satisfies
p^{2}\nmid\frac{L_{0}}{(\ell_{2},d_{2})}=\alpha_{1}.

9.2.
Combinatorics and computations of sums over primes

By Remark
3
, we can apply Lemma
9.2
to
F_{L_{0},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)
. Recall that
\mathfrak{p}(K)
,
L_{1}
,
L_{2}
are pairwise relatively prime,
c_{1}d_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)
,
\ell_{1}|L_{1}
,
\ell_{2}|L_{2}
