and
c_{2}d_{2}|\ell_{\infty}^{2}|L_{2}^{\infty}
. In this section, we use the notation that
a\cdot b
means the usual multiplication of integers
a
and
b
with
(a,b)=1
, which is useful to follow the arguments.

With
r=d_{1}\cdot d_{2}
,
\alpha=L_{1}\ell_{1}\cdot\ell_{2}
,
r_{1}=d_{1}\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})}
,
\alpha_{1}=\ell_{1}^{2}\cdot\frac{L_{1}}{\ell_{1}}\cdot\frac{\ell_{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{1}=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}d_{1}}
,
\mathfrak{m}_{2}=\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{c_{2}d_{2}}
and
\mathfrak{m}=\mathfrak{m}_{1}\cdot\mathfrak{m}_{2}
, we find that

\displaystyle F_{L_{0},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=

\displaystyle\zeta(1+s)\widetilde{F}(s,it)\frac{(d_{2},\ell_{2})^{1+s}}{(d_{1}d_{2})^{1+s}}\prod_{p|d_{1}\cdot d_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}}{p^{s}(p-1)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p|\ell_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p}{p-1}}}\right)\prod_{p|\frac{L_{1}}{\ell_{1}}\cdot\frac{\ell_{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}+\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{s+1}(p-1)}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{m}\\
p\nmid d_{1}\cdot L_{1}\cdot\ell_{2}d_{2}\end{subarray}}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}}\cdot L_{1}\cdot\frac{\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})c_{2}}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}.

Since
(d_{1},\mathfrak{m})=1
and
(L_{1},\mathfrak{m})=1
, the above products can be split as

\displaystyle\prod_{p|d_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|d_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{2}}}{p^{s}(p-1)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p|\ell_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p}{p-1}}}\right)\prod_{p|\frac{L_{1}}{\ell_{1}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2+s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|\frac{\ell_{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}+\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+s}(p-1)}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p|\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{m}_{2}\\
p\nmid\ell_{2}d_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p|\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}}\cdot L_{1}\cdot\frac{\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})c_{2}}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}.

We want to separate the primes dividing
\mathfrak{p}(K)
from the others. Define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{J}_{1}(s,

\displaystyle z;\mathbb{L},c_{2},d_{2}):=\frac{(d_{2},\ell_{2})^{1+s}}{d_{2}^{1+s}}\prod_{p|d_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{2}}}{p^{s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|\ell_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p}{p-1}}}\right)

(9.14)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{p|\frac{L_{1}}{\ell_{1}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{p^{2+s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|\frac{\ell_{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}+\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+s}(p-1)}}}\right)}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{m}_{2}\\
p\nmid\ell_{2}d_{2}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,z)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|L_{1}\cdot\frac{\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})c_{2}}}W_{p}(s,z)^{-1}

for
\mathbb{L}
as in (
5.5
) and
\mathfrak{m}_{2}=\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{c_{2}d_{2}}
, then we have

(9.15)

\begin{split}F_{L_{0},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=&\zeta(1+s)\widetilde{F}(s,it)\frac{\mathfrak{J}_{1}(s,it;\mathbb{L},c_{2},d_{2})}{d_{1}^{1+s}}\prod_{p|d_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+s}(p-1)}}}\right)\\
&\times\prod_{p|\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}\prod_{p|\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}d_{1}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right).\end{split}

Note that we will need the following special values to compute the off-diagonal main terms.

Lemma 9.3
.

We have

\widetilde{F}_{0}(-1,z)=1,

W_{p}(-1,z)^{-1}=1-\frac{1}{p}.

Moreover,

\mathfrak{J}_{1}(-1,z;\mathbb{L},c_{2},d_{2})=\delta_{c_{2}d_{2}=\ell_{\infty}^{2}}.

Proof.

We only compute
\mathfrak{J}_{1}(-1,z;\mathbb{L},c_{2},d_{2})
when
c_{2}d_{2}\neq\ell_{\infty}^{2}
, since the other cases are straightforward from definitions.
If
(d_{2},m_{2})\neq 1
, then the product over
p|d_{2}
in (
9.14
) at
s=-1
is
0
. If
(\frac{\ell_{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})},\mathfrak{m}_{2})\neq 1
, then the product over
p|\frac{\ell_{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})}
in (
9.14
) at
s=-1
is
0
. Hence, the remaining case is that
(d_{2}\ell_{2},\mathfrak{m}_{2})=1
and
\mathfrak{m}_{2}\neq 1
. In this case, the product over
p|\mathfrak{m}_{2}
and
p\nmid\ell_{2}d_{2}
in (
9.14
) at
s=-1
is
0
. Thus, we have
\mathfrak{J}_{1}(-1,z;\mathbb{L},c_{2},d_{2})=0
when
\mathfrak{m}_{2}\neq 1
.
∎

Define

(9.16)

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{J}_{2}(s,z;\mathbb{L}):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{2},d_{2}\\
c_{2}d_{2}|\ell_{\infty}^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d_{2})(c_{2}d_{2})^{2z}}{\ell_{\infty}^{2z}}\frac{\mathfrak{J}_{1}(s,z;\mathbb{L},c_{2},d_{2})}{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\end{split}

for
\mathbb{L}
as in (
5.5
), then by (
9.14
) we have

(9.17)

\mathfrak{J}_{2}(-s,z;\mathbb{L})\ll\frac{(L_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{\epsilon}}{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}

for any
\epsilon>0
and for
-\frac{10}{\log Q}\leq\textup{Re}(s)\leq 1+\frac{10}{\log Q}
and
|\textup{Re}(z)|\leq\frac{10}{\log Q}
.
By Lemma
9.1
, (
9.15
) and (
9.16
), we find that

\widetilde{\varrho}_{L_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s)=\frac{\zeta(1+s)\widetilde{F}(s,it)}{|\zeta(1+2it)|^{2}}\mathfrak{J}_{2}(s,it;\mathbb{L})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{1},d_{1}\\
c_{1}d_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d_{1})\mathfrak{p}(K)^{it}}{c_{1}^{2it}d_{1}^{1+s+2it}}\\
\times\prod_{p|d_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+s}(p-1)}}}\right)\frac{1}{W_{p}(s,it)}}}\right)\prod_{p|\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}d_{1}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}+\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\frac{1}{W_{p}(s,it)}}}\right).

We can switch the order of integrals and sums in (
9.5
) and integrate the
\xi
-integral first by Lemma
2.19
.
Next, applying the above and Lemma
9.2
and substituting
it=z
, we find that

(9.18)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\mathfrak{M}_{1}(-\epsilon_{1},0;Q^{s}\mathfrak{K}_{1,L_{1}L_{2}}(K:s,z)),

where

(9.19)

\mathfrak{M}_{1}(c_{s},c_{z};\mathfrak{K}(s,z)):=\frac{i^{-k}}{2}\int_{(c_{z})}\int_{(c_{s})}\widetilde{\Psi}(s)\widetilde{F}_{0}(-s,z)\frac{\mathfrak{J}_{2}(-s,z;\mathbb{L})}{(4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{s}}\\
\times\frac{\zeta(1-s)\zeta(2-s)\zeta(2+2z-s)\zeta(2-2z-s)}{\zeta(1+2z)\zeta(1-2z)\sin(\pi z)}\mathfrak{K}(s,z)\\
\times 2^{s-1}\Gamma(1-s)(\mathcal{G}_{2z,k-1}(s)-\mathcal{G}_{-2z,k-1}(s))dsdz

and

(9.20)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{K}_{1,L_{1}L_{2}}(K:s,z):=&\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{p_{k_{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}(1+s)}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\\
&\times\sum_{c_{1}d_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)}\frac{\mu(d_{1})\mathfrak{p}(K)^{z}}{c_{1}^{2z}d_{1}^{1-s+2z}}\prod_{p|d_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1-s}(p-1)}}}\right)\frac{1}{W_{p}(-s,z)}}}\right)\\
&\times\prod_{p|\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}d_{1}}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p^{1-s}}+\frac{1}{p^{2-2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\frac{1}{W_{p}(-s,it)}}}\right).\end{split}

We compute the integrals in (
9.19
) by residue calculus. We want to shift the
s
-contour to
\textup{Re}(s)\approx 1
.
By Lemma
2.19
, we have

(9.21)

\frac{\Gamma(1-s)\mathcal{G}_{\pm 2z,k-1}(s)}{\sin(\pi z)}\ll(1+|\textup{Im}(s)|)^{\sigma-\frac{5}{2}}(1+|\textup{Im}(z)|)^{2\sigma-2},

where
\sigma=\textup{Re}(s)
. We shift the
z
-contour to
\textup{Re}(z)=\frac{1}{\log Q}
and the
s
-contour to
\textup{Re}(s)=\frac{4}{\log Q}
to find that

(9.22)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\mathfrak{M}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4}{\log Q},\frac{1}{\log Q};Q^{s}\mathfrak{K}_{1,L_{1}L_{2}}(K:s,z)}}\right).

Here, the residue at
s=0
vanishes by (
2.18
).
Since the
z
-integral may not be convergent when
\sigma\geq 1/2
by (
9.22
), we cannot move the
s
-contour to the right of
1/2
. To overcome this difficulty, we will find small terms in
\mathfrak{K}_{1,L_{1}L_{2}}(K:s,z)
such that we can shift the
s
-contour to the right of
1/2
except for the small terms.

By letting
c_{1}=\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})
,
d_{1}=\mathfrak{p}(K_{2})
and
\mathfrak{p}(K_{3})=\mathfrak{p}(K)/c_{1}d_{1}
for
K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K

in (
9.20
), we have

(9.23)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{K}_{1,L_{1}L_{2}}(K:s,z)&=\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K}(-1)^{|K_{2}|}\mathfrak{K}_{2,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K}:s,z),\\
\mathfrak{K}_{2,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K}:s,z)&:=\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1,2,3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{i}}\mathcal{P}_{i,k_{j}}(p_{k_{j}},s,z)}}\right)\end{split}

for
\mathbb{K}:=(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3})
, where

(9.24)

\begin{split}\mathcal{P}_{1,k_{j}}(p,s,z)&:=\frac{\log p}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p}{\log Q}}}\right),\\
\mathcal{P}_{2,k_{j}}(p,s,z)&:=\frac{\log p}{p^{\frac{3}{2}-\frac{s}{2}+z}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1-s}(p-1)}}}\right)\frac{1}{W_{p}(-s,z)}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p}{\log Q}}}\right),\\
\mathcal{P}_{3,k_{j}}(p,s,z)&:=\frac{\log p}{p^{\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p^{1-s}}+\frac{1}{p^{2-2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\frac{1}{W_{p}(-s,z)}}}\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p}{\log Q}}}\right).\end{split}

By Lemma
2.14
, we have

(9.25)

\mathfrak{K}_{2,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K}:s,z)=\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K}}\mu^{*}(\underline{G})\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z),

where

(9.26)

\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p\\
(p,L_{1}L_{2})=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1,2,3}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{i}\cap G_{j}}\mathcal{P}_{i,k_{j}}(p,s,z)}}\right).

By Lemmas
2.9
and
2.13
, it is straightforward to estimate
\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)
as follows.

Lemma 9.4
.

Assume RH. Let
s,z
be complex numbers satisfying
\textup{Re}(s)=\frac{4}{\log Q}
and
\textup{Re}(z)=\frac{1}{\log Q}
.
If
|G_{j}|\geq 2
, then

\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)\ll(\log Q)^{2}.

If
G_{j}=\{k_{j}\}
, then we have

\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)=\begin{cases}\Phi_{k_{j}}(-i\mathcal{U}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{s}{2}-z))\log Q+O((\log Q)^{2})&\textup{ if }k_{j}\in K_{1},\\
O(1)&\textup{ if }k_{j}\in K_{2},\\
\Phi_{k_{j}}(-i\mathcal{U}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{s}{2}+z))\log Q+O((\log Q)^{2})&\textup{ if }k_{j}\in K_{3},\end{cases}

where
\mathcal{U}=\frac{\log Q}{2\pi}
.

The above lemma readily implies the following corollary.

Corollary 9.5
.

Assume RH. Let
\Pi_{K,E,1}
be the set of
\underline{G}=\{G_{1},\ldots,G_{\nu}\}\in\Pi_{K}
such that
G_{j}\subset K_{2}
whenever
|G_{j}|=1
. Then we have

\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K,E,1}}\mu^{*}(\underline{G})\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)\ll(\log Q)^{|K|}

for
\textup{Re}(s)=\frac{4}{\log Q}
and
\textup{Re}(z)=\frac{1}{\log Q}
.

Let
\Pi^{\prime}_{K}:=\Pi_{K}\setminus\Pi_{K,E,1}
. Then
\underline{G}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}
means that there exists
G_{j}\in\underline{G}
such that
|G_{j}|=1
and
G_{j}\subset K_{1}\sqcup K_{3}
. Motivated from Lemma
9.4
, for
G_{j}=\{k_{j}\}
we let

(9.27)

\frac{\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)}{\log Q}:=\begin{cases}\Phi_{k_{j}}(-i\mathcal{U}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{s}{2}-z))&\textup{ if }G_{j}\subset K_{1},\\
\Phi_{k_{j}}(-i\mathcal{U}(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{s}{2}-z))&\textup{ if }G_{j}\subset K_{2},\\
\Phi_{k_{j}}(-i\mathcal{U}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{s}{2}+z))&\textup{ if }G_{j}\subset K_{3},\end{cases}

(9.28)

\frac{\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)}{\log Q}:=\begin{cases}-\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}-z)w}dw&\textup{ if }G_{j}\subset K_{1},\\
-\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z)w}dw&\textup{ if }G_{j}\subset K_{2},\end{cases}

\frac{\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)}{\log Q}:=-\Phi_{k_{j}}(-i\mathcal{U}(-\tfrac{1}{2}+\tfrac{s}{2}+z))+\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)(Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z)w}-Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}+z)w})dw

if
G_{j}\subset K_{3}
,
and

(9.29)

\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},E}(s,z):=\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)-\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z).

Then we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 9.6
.

Assume RH. Let
s,z
be complex numbers satisfying
\textup{Re}(s)=\frac{4}{\log Q}
and
\textup{Re}(z)=\frac{1}{\log Q}
.
Define

K_{\textup{s13}}:=K_{\textup{s13}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K}):=\bigcup_{\begin{subarray}{c}G_{j}\in\underline{G}\\
|G_{j}|=1,\penalty\ G_{j}\subset K_{1}\sqcup K_{3}\end{subarray}}G_{j},

then
K_{\textup{s13}}\neq\emptyset
for each
\underline{G}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}
and

\mathfrak{K}_{2,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K}:s,z)=\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}}\mu^{*}(\underline{G})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{M}\sqcup K_{E}=K_{\textup{s13}}\\
K_{M}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)+O((\log Q)^{2|K|}),

where

(9.30)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z):=&\prod_{G_{j}\subset K_{M}}\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)\prod_{G_{j}\subset K_{E}}\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},E}(s,z)\\
&\times\prod_{G_{j}\not\subset K_{\textup{s13}}}\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z).\end{split}

Proof.

By (
9.25
) and Corollary
9.5
, we have

\mathfrak{K}_{2,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K}:s,z)=\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}}\mu^{*}(\underline{G})\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)+O((\log Q)^{|K|}).

For each
\underline{G}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}
, then we have

\prod_{j=1}^{\nu}\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)=\prod_{G_{j}\subset K_{\textup{s13}}}(\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)+\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},E}(s,z))\prod_{G_{j}\not\subset K_{\textup{s13}}}\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)\\
=\sum_{K_{M}\sqcup K_{E}=K_{\textup{s13}}}\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z).

By Lemmas
2.13
and
9.4
,

\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)=O((\log Q)^{2|K|})
when
K_{M}=\emptyset
. This proves the lemma.
∎

By collecting the above results we have the following lemma.

Lemma 9.7
.

Assuming RH and (
5.6
), we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K}(-1)^{|K_{2}|}\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}}\mu^{*}(\underline{G})\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{M}\sqcup K_{E}=K_{\textup{s13}}\\
K_{M}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E})+O(Q^{\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
, where

(9.31)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}):=\mathfrak{M}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4}{\log Q},\frac{1}{\log Q};Q^{s}\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)}}\right)

with
\mathfrak{M}_{1}
defined in (
9.19
).

Proof.

By (
9.17
), (
9.22
), (
9.23
) and Lemma
9.6
, it is enough to show that

\int_{(\frac{1}{\log Q})}\int_{(\frac{4}{\log Q})}\frac{|\widetilde{\Psi}(s)\zeta(1-s)|}{|\zeta(1+2z)\zeta(1-2z)\sin(\pi z)|}(\log Q)^{2|K|}\\
\times|\Gamma(1-s)(\mathcal{G}_{2z,k-1}(s)-\mathcal{G}_{-2z,k-1}(s))||ds||dz|\ll Q^{\epsilon}

for any
\epsilon>0
. This can be easily justified by the following inequalities.

By repeated integration by parts, we have
\widetilde{\Psi}(s)\ll\frac{1}{|s|(1+|s|)^{A}}

for any
A\geq 0
. We also have an upper bound for

\big|\frac{\Gamma(1-s)\mathcal{G}_{\pm 2z,k-1}(s)}{\sin(\pi z)}\big|

in (
9.21
). Together with well-known bounds for the Riemann zeta function near
\textup{Re}(s)=1
, these bounds are sufficient to justify the lemma.
∎

By Lemma
9.7
, we next compute the integral

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E})

for
\underline{G}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}
and
K_{M}\neq\emptyset
.
Each
\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)
in (
9.30
) has a factor
\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)
defined in (
9.27
), which is rapidly decreasing as
|\textup{Im}(\frac{s}{2}\pm z)|\to\infty
on given vertical lines of
s
and
z
. Since
\widetilde{\Psi}(s)
is also rapidly decreasing as
|\textup{Im}(s)|\to\infty
, the convergence issue has been resolved. We can now move the
s
-contour in (
9.31
) to
\textup{Re}(s)=1-\frac{4}{\log Q}
and see that

(9.32)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E})=\mathfrak{M}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{4}{\log Q},\frac{1}{\log Q};Q^{s}\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)}}\right).

To estimate
\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)
, we find asymptotic formulas for
\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)
.

Lemma 9.8
.

Assume RH. Let
s,z
be complex numbers that satisfy
\textup{Re}(s)=1-\frac{4}{\log Q}
and
\textup{Re}(z)=\frac{1}{\log Q}
.
If
|G_{j}|\geq 2
, then

\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K}}(s,z)\ll 1.

If
|G_{j}|=1
, then

\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},2}(s,z):=\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},E}(s,z)-\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)\ll 1+|s|+|z|.

Moreover,
\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)\ll\log Q
and
\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},E}(s,z)\ll\log Q+|s|+|z|
, when
|G_{j}|=1
.

The proof is straightforward from Lemmas
2.13
and
2.22
.
The above lemma readily implies the following corollary.

Corollary 9.9
.

Assume RH. Let
\underline{G}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}
and assume that
|G_{j}|\geq 2
for some
G_{j}\in\underline{G}
. Then we have

\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)\ll(\log Q+|s|+|z|)^{|K|-2}\prod_{G_{j}\subset K_{M}}\frac{|\mathfrak{P}_{G_{j};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)|}{\log Q}

for
\textup{Re}(s)=1-\frac{4}{\log Q}
and
\textup{Re}(z)=\frac{1}{\log Q}
.

Let
\Pi_{K,E,2}
be the set of
\underline{G}=\{G_{1},\ldots,G_{\nu}\}\in\Pi^{\prime}_{K}
such that
|G_{j}|\geq 2
for some
j\leq\nu
, then
\Pi^{\prime}_{K}=\{\pi_{K,1}\}\sqcup\Pi_{K,E,2}
, where

\pi_{K,1}=\{\{k\}|k\in K\}
. To show that the contribution of
\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K,E,2}
is small, we need a technical lemma as follows.

Lemma 9.10
.

Let
\Phi
be an even Schwartz function with its Fourier transform compactly supported. Define

(9.33)

|\mathfrak{M}_{1}|(c_{s},c_{z};\mathfrak{K}(s,z)):=\frac{1}{(L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{c_{s}}}\int_{(c_{z})}\int_{(c_{s})}|\widetilde{\Psi}(s)\widetilde{F}_{0}(-s,z)\mathfrak{J}_{2}(-s,z;\mathbb{L})|\\
\times\frac{|\zeta(1-s)\zeta(2-s)\zeta(2+2z-s)\zeta(2-2z-s)|}{|\zeta(1+2z)\zeta(1-2z)\sin(\pi z)|}\mathfrak{K}(s,z)\\
\times|\Phi(-i\mathcal{U}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{s}{2}\pm z))\Gamma(1-s)(\mathcal{G}_{2z,k-1}(s)-\mathcal{G}_{-2z,k-1}(s))||ds||dz|.

Let
c_{s}=1-\frac{4}{\log Q}
,
c_{z}=\frac{1}{\log Q}
and
A_{0}\geq 0
, then we have

|\mathfrak{M}_{1}|(c_{s},c_{z};1+|s|^{A_{0}}+|z|^{A_{0}})\ll\frac{(L_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{\epsilon}}{(L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{c_{s}}L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\log\log Q

for any
\epsilon>0
.

Proof.

Let
\textup{Re}(s)=1-\frac{4}{\log Q}
and
\textup{Re}(z)=\frac{1}{\log Q}
. By repeated integration by parts, we have
\widetilde{\Psi}(s)\ll|s|^{-A}
. Also, by Lemma
2.22
,
\Phi(-i\mathcal{U}(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{s}{2}\pm z))\ll(1+|\textup{Im}(s\mp 2z)|\log Q)^{-A}
for any
A\geq 0
.
By these bounds, (
9.17
) and Lemmas
2.19
and
9.2
, it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{1}:=

\displaystyle\int_{(c_{z})}\int_{(c_{s})}\frac{(1+|s|^{A_{0}}+|z|^{A_{0}})|\zeta(2-s)\zeta(2+2z-s)\zeta(2-2z-s)|e^{\pi|\textup{Im}(z)|}}{|s|^{A}(1+|\textup{Im}(s\mp 2z)|\log Q)^{A}|\zeta(1+2z)\zeta(1-2z)\sin(\pi z)|}|ds||dz|

\displaystyle\ll\log\log Q

for some
A>0
. We only consider the minus case of
\textup{Im}(s\mp 2z)
, since the other case holds by the same way.

By the bounds

\frac{e^{\pi|\textup{Im}(z)|}}{|\zeta(1+2z)\zeta(1-2z)\sin(\pi z)|}\ll\frac{1}{\log Q}+|y|,

\zeta(2-s)\ll\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\log Q}+|t|}+|t|^{\epsilon},\qquad\zeta(2\pm 2z-s)\ll\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\log Q}+|t\mp 2y|}+|t\mp 2y|^{\epsilon}

for
z=\frac{1}{\log Q}+iy
and
s=1-\frac{4}{\log Q}+it
, we find that

\mathfrak{I}_{1}\ll\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{(1+|t|+|y|)^{A_{0}}}{(1+|t|)^{A}(1+|t-2y|\log Q)^{A}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\log Q}+|y|}}\right)\\
\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\log Q}+|t|}+|t|^{\epsilon}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\log Q}+|t-2y|}+|t-2y|^{\epsilon}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{\frac{1}{\log Q}+|t+2y|}+|t+2y|^{\epsilon}}}\right)dtdy

for any
\epsilon>0
. By substituting
t,y
to
\frac{t}{\log Q}
,
\frac{y}{\log Q}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{I}_{1}\ll

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{(1+\frac{|t|+|y|}{\log Q})^{A_{0}}(1+|y|)}{(1+\frac{|t|}{\log Q})^{A}(1+|t-2y|)^{A}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{1+|t|}+\frac{|t|^{\epsilon}}{(\log Q)^{1+\epsilon}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{1+|t-2y|}+\frac{|t-2y|^{\epsilon}}{(\log Q)^{1+\epsilon}}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{1+|t+2y|}+\frac{|t+2y|^{\epsilon}}{(\log Q)^{1+\epsilon}}}}\right)dtdy.

Because of the factor
(1+\frac{|t|}{\log Q})^{A}(1+|t-2y|)^{A}
in the denominator with any choice of
A>0
, we expect that the main contribution comes from the region
|t|\ll\log Q
and
|t-2y|\ll 1
. The integral over this region is bounded by

\begin{split}\int_{|t|<\log Q}\int_{|t-2y|\ll 1}(1+|y|)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{1+|t|}}}\right)\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{1+|t+2y|}}}\right)\>dy\>dt\ll\log\log Q.\end{split}

Here, the inequality
1+|y|\leq(1+|t-2y|)(1+|t+2y|)
may be useful.
∎

Now we are ready to show that the contribution of
\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K,E,2}
is small, so that only
\pi_{K,1}
contributes.

Lemma 9.11
.

Assuming RH and (
5.6
), we have

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K}(-1)^{|K_{2}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{M}\sqcup K_{E}=K_{\textup{s13}}\\
K_{M}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}(\pi_{K,1},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E})

\displaystyle+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q(\log Q)^{|K|-2}\log\log Q}{L_{1}^{2-\epsilon}L_{2}\ell_{1}(\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{1-\epsilon}}}}\right)

for any
\epsilon>0
, where
\mathbb{K}=(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3})
and
K_{\textup{s13}}=K_{1}\sqcup K_{3}
.

Proof.

By Lemma
9.7
, (
9.32
) and the definition of
\Pi_{K,E,2}
above Lemma
9.10
, it suffices to show that

\mathfrak{M}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{4}{\log Q},\frac{1}{\log Q};Q^{s}\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\underline{G},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)}}\right)\ll\frac{Q(\log Q)^{|K|-2}\log\log Q}{L_{1}^{2-\epsilon}L_{2}\ell_{1}(\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{1-\epsilon}}

for
\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K,E,2}
,
K_{M}\sqcup K_{E}=K_{1}\sqcup K_{3}
and
K_{M}\neq\emptyset
. It follows from (
9.19
), Corollary
9.9
and Lemma
9.10
. ∎

To compute
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}(\pi_{K,1},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E})
in Lemma
9.11
, we see that

(9.34)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}&(\pi_{K,1},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)\\
=&\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{M}}\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{E}}(\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)+O(1+|s|+|z|))\\
&\times\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}(\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)+\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)+O(1+|s|+|z|))\end{split}

by (
9.30
) and Lemma
9.8
.
Since
\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)\ll\log Q
and
\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)\ll\log Q
, we expect that the contribution of the
O
-terms in (
9.34
) is small. This is justified in the next lemma.

Lemma 9.12
.

Assuming RH and (
5.6
), we have

(9.35)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\mathfrak{M}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{4}{\log Q},\frac{1}{\log Q};Q^{s}\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(s,z)}}\right)+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q(\log Q)^{|K|-1}\log\log Q}{L_{1}^{2-\epsilon}L_{2}\ell_{1}(\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{1-\epsilon}}}}\right)

for any
\epsilon>0
,
where
\mathfrak{M}_{1}
is defined in (
9.19
) and

(9.36)

\begin{split}&\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(s,z)(\log Q)^{-|K|}\\
=&\prod_{k_{j}\in K}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)(Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}-z)w}-Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z)w}+Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}+z)w}-Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z)w})dw\\
&-\prod_{k_{j}\in K}\bigg(-2\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)(Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z)w}+Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z)w})dw\bigg).\end{split}

Proof.

Define

(9.37)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z):=&\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{M}}\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{E}}\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)\\
&\times\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}(\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)+\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z))\end{split}

for
\mathbb{K}=(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3})
.
By expanding the product (
9.34
), we find that

\mathfrak{K}_{3,L_{1}L_{2}}(\pi_{K,1},\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)-\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)\\
\ll\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left((1+|s|+|z|)\log Q}}\right)^{|K|-1}\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{M}}\frac{|\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)|}{\log Q},

which is similar to the bound in Corollary
9.9
, but essentially
\log Q
larger. By Lemma
9.10
and the above inequality, (
9.35
) holds with

(9.38)

\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(s,z):=\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K}(-1)^{|K_{2}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{M}\sqcup K_{E}=K_{\textup{s13}}\\
K_{M}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z).

Next, we compute
\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(s,z)
. The inner sum in (
9.38
) equals to

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{M}\sqcup K_{E}=K_{1}\sqcup K_{3}}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K},K_{M},K_{E}:s,z)-\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(\mathbb{K},\emptyset,K_{\textup{s13}}:s,z)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{k_{j}\in K}(\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)+\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z))

\displaystyle-\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{1}\sqcup K_{3}}\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}(\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)+\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)).

By the definitions (
9.27
) and (
9.28
) and the fact that each
\Phi_{k_{j}}
is even, we find that

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K}\frac{(-1)^{|K_{2}|}}{(\log Q)^{|K|}}\prod_{k_{j}\in K}(\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)+\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K}\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{1}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}-z)w}dw\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z)w}dw}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)(Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}+z)w}-Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z)w})dw

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{k_{j}\in K}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)(Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}-z)w}-Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z)w}+Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}+z)w}-Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z)w})dw

and similarly

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K}\frac{(-1)^{|K_{2}|}}{(\log Q)^{|K|}}\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{1}\sqcup K_{3}}\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}(\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},0}(s,z)+\mathfrak{P}_{\{k_{j}\};\mathbb{K},1}(s,z))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K}\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}-z)w}dw}}\right)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z)w}dw}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{3}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(\frac{1}{2}-\frac{s}{2}+z)w}dw-\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z)w}dw}}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\prod_{k_{j}\in K}\bigg(-2\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z)w}dw-2\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{(-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z)w}dw\bigg).

These equations imply (
9.36
).
∎

Next we perform the change of variables
u=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}+z
and
v=-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{s}{2}-z
, or equivalently
s=u+v+1
and
z=\frac{1}{2}(u-v)
, then we have

(9.39)

\mathfrak{M}_{1}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1-\frac{4}{\log Q},\frac{1}{\log Q};Q^{s}\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(s,z)}}\right)\\
=\frac{Q(\log Q)^{|K|}}{L_{1}L_{2}e}\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};Q^{u+v}\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v)}}\right)

and

(9.40)

\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(c_{u},c_{v};\mathfrak{K}(u,v)}}\right):=\frac{i^{-k}}{8\pi}\int_{(c_{v})}\int_{(c_{u})}\widetilde{\Psi}(u+v+1)\mathfrak{J}_{3}(u,v;\mathbb{L})\\
\times\frac{\zeta(-u-v)\zeta(1-u-v)\zeta(1-2v)\zeta(1-2u)}{\zeta(1+u-v)\zeta(1-u+v)\sin(\frac{\pi}{2}(u-v))}\mathfrak{K}(u,v)\\
\times\Gamma(-u-v)(\mathcal{G}_{u-v,k-1}(u+v+1)-\mathcal{G}_{v-u,k-1}(u+v+1))dudv,

where

(9.41)

\mathfrak{J}_{3}(u,v;\mathbb{L}):=\widetilde{F}_{0}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-u-v-1,\frac{u-v}{2}}}\right)\frac{\mathfrak{J}_{2}(-u-v-1,\frac{u-v}{2};\mathbb{L})}{(2\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{u+v}}

and

\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v):=\mathfrak{K}_{4,L_{1}L_{2}}(u+v+1,\tfrac{1}{2}(u-v))(\log Q)^{-|K|}.

Note that by Lemma
9.2
, (
9.14
), (
9.16
) and (
9.41
), we have

(9.42)

\mathfrak{J}_{3}(u,v;\mathbb{L})\ll\frac{(L_{1}\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{\epsilon}(1+\ell_{\infty}^{\textup{Re}(v-u)})}{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}(L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{\textup{Re}(u+v)}}.

Since

\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}u)=\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{uw}dw+\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)Q^{-uw}dw
, by (
9.36
) with the substitution to
u,v
,
we find that

(9.43)

\begin{split}\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v)&=\prod_{k_{j}\in K}\bigg(\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}u)+\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}v)-2\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)(Q^{uw}+Q^{vw})dw\bigg)\\
&\qquad-\prod_{k_{j}\in K}\bigg(-2\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)(Q^{uw}+Q^{vw})dw\bigg)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K\\
K_{3}\neq K\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}:u,v),\end{split}

where

(9.44)

\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}:u,v)\\
:=\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{1}}\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}u)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}v)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{3}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-2\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w)(Q^{uw}+Q^{vw})dw}}\right).

We now analyze
\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};Q^{u+v}\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v)}}\right)
. As an analogue of the
P_{1}^{it}P_{2}^{-it}
structure discussed in the introduction, we show that the main contribution comes from
\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}:u,v)
with
K_{1},K_{2}\neq\emptyset
in (
9.43
).
In this case,
\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}:u,v)
contains both factors
\Phi_{\ell_{1}}(i\mathcal{U}u)
and
\Phi_{\ell_{2}}(i\mathcal{U}v)

for some
\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}
. Hence, define

(9.45)

\mathfrak{K}_{6,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K\\
K_{1},K_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}:u,v),

then we justify the above discussion.

Lemma 9.13
.

Assuming RH and (
5.6
), we have

(9.46)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\frac{Q(\log Q)^{|K|}}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},1}+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q(\log Q)^{|K|-1}\log\log Q}{L_{1}^{2-\epsilon}L_{2}\ell_{1}(\ell_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{1-\epsilon}}}}\right)

for any
\epsilon>0
, where

(9.47)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},1}:=\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};Q^{u+v}\mathfrak{K}_{6,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v)}}\right).

Proof.

By Lemma
9.12
and (
9.39
), it is enough to show that

\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};Q^{u+v}(\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v)-\mathfrak{K}_{6,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v))}}\right)\ll\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{\epsilon}}{L_{1}^{1-\epsilon}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}^{1-\epsilon}}\frac{\log\log Q}{\log Q}.

By (
9.43
) and (
9.45
),

\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v)-\mathfrak{K}_{6,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v)
is the sum of
\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(K_{1},K_{2},K_{3}:u,v)
over
K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K
,
K_{3}\neq K
and
K_{1}
or
K_{2}=\emptyset
.
So it suffices to show that

(9.48)

\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};Q^{u+v}\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(\emptyset,K_{2},K_{3}:u,v)}}\right)\ll\frac{\ell_{\infty}^{\epsilon}}{L_{1}^{1-\epsilon}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}^{1-\epsilon}}\frac{\log\log Q}{\log Q}

for
K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=K
and
K_{2}\neq\emptyset
. The case
K_{2}=\emptyset
would hold similarly.

By shifting the
u
-contour to
\textup{Re}(u)=-\frac{1}{7}
and then applying Lemma
2.19
and

\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k}(w)(Q^{uw}+Q^{vw})\>dw\ll\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k}(w)(Q^{-w/7}+e^{-3w})\>dw\ll 1,

we find that

\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};Q^{u+v}\mathfrak{K}_{5,L_{1}L_{2}}(\emptyset,K_{2},K_{3}:u,v)}}\right)\\
\ll Q^{-\frac{1}{7}}\int_{(\frac{-3}{\log Q})}\int_{(\frac{-1}{7})}\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}|\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}v)|\frac{|\mathfrak{J}_{3}(u,v;\mathbb{L})|}{|u+v|^{A}}\frac{|\zeta(1-2v)\zeta(1-2u)||du||dv|}{|\zeta(1+u-v)\zeta(1-u+v)|}\ll Q^{\frac{-1}{7}+\epsilon}

for any
\epsilon>0
. This implies (
9.48
).
∎

In the next section, we compute
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},1}
and conclude the proof of Proposition
8.2
.

10.
Residue calculation: computation of
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},1}

In this section, assuming (
5.6
) for
\mathbb{L}
, we compute
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},1}
defined in (
9.47
). By (
9.44
) and (
9.45
), we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{K}_{6,L_{1}L_{2}}(u,v)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}=K\\
K_{1},K_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}(-2)^{|K_{3}|+|K_{4}|}\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{1}}\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}u)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{2}}\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}v)

\displaystyle\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w_{k_{j}})Q^{uw_{k_{j}}}dw_{k_{j}}\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{4}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w_{k_{j}})Q^{vw_{k_{j}}}dw_{k_{j}}.

By Fourier inversion and the change of variables, we have

\Phi_{K_{1}}(i\mathcal{U}u):=\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{1}}\Phi_{k_{j}}(i\mathcal{U}u)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})Q^{-u(t_{1}+W_{1})}dt_{1},

and after integrating by parts twice

\Phi_{K_{2}}(i\mathcal{U}v)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})\frac{Q^{-v(t_{2}+W_{2})}}{v^{2}(\log Q)^{2}}dt_{2},

where

W_{1}:=1+\sum_{k_{j}\in K_{3}}w_{k_{j}},\qquad W_{2}:=1+\sum_{k_{j}\in K_{4}}w_{k_{j}}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle Q^{u+v}\mathfrak{K}_{6,L_{1}L_{2}}

\displaystyle(u,v)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}=K\\
K_{1},K_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\frac{(-2)^{|K_{3}|+|K_{4}|}}{(\log Q)^{2}}\int_{[0,\infty)^{|K_{3}|+|K_{4}|}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}

\displaystyle\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w_{k_{j}})dw_{k_{j}}}}\right).

The reason we had
\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2})
is that the factor
\frac{1}{v^{2}}
provides an absolute convergence of the integrals in
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},1}
so that we can change their orders. We obtain

(10.1)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},1}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}=K\\
K_{1},K_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\frac{(-2)^{|K_{3}|+|K_{4}|}}{(\log Q)^{2}}\\
\int_{[0,\infty)^{|K_{3}|+|K_{4}|}}\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},2}(W_{1},W_{2})\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w_{k_{j}})dw_{k_{j}}}}\right),

where

(10.2)

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},2}(W_{1},W_{2}):=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}}}\right)\\
\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}.

The factor
Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}
essentially determines the size of
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},2}
. Depending on whether
t_{1}
is positive or negative, we shift the
u
-integral to the right or left, respectively, so that the power of
Q
becomes smaller after the shifts. We do the same for
t_{2}
and the
v
-integral. Then we expect to collect residues when we shift the integrals to the right and the resulting integrals are expected to be small. These observations are justified in the following two lemmas.

Lemma 10.1
.

Assume (
5.6
) and
|W_{1}|,|W_{2}|\leq W
for some
W>0
, then we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},2}(W_{1},W_{2})=\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}}}\right)\\
\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}+O((\log Q)^{1+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
.

Proof.

It is enough to estimate the integral in (
10.2
) for
t_{1}\leq 0
or
t_{2}\leq 0
.
For
t_{1}\leq 0
, we first shift the
u
-contour in (
9.40
) to
\textup{Re}(u)=-\epsilon_{1}
and then change the order of integrals. We find that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\epsilon_{1},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}}}\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\epsilon_{1},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{Q^{-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}Q^{-ut_{1}}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})dt_{1}}}\right)\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2}.

After the
t_{1}
-integration by parts, we find that the above is

\displaystyle\ll|\mathfrak{M}_{2}|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\epsilon_{1},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{1}{|v|^{2}|u|\log Q}}}\right),

where

(10.3)

|\mathfrak{M}_{2}|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(c_{u},c_{v};\mathfrak{K}(u,v)}}\right):=\int_{(c_{v})}\int_{(c_{u})}|\widetilde{\Psi}(u+v+1)\mathfrak{J}_{3}(u,v;\mathbb{L})|\\
\frac{|\zeta(-u-v)\zeta(1-u-v)\zeta(1-2v)\zeta(1-2u)|}{|\zeta(1+u-v)\zeta(1-u+v)\sin(\frac{\pi}{2}(u-v))|}\mathfrak{K}(u,v)\\
|\Gamma(-u-v)(\mathcal{G}_{u-v,k-1}(u+v+1)-\mathcal{G}_{v-u,k-1}(u+v+1))||du||dv|.

By Lemma
2.19
and (
9.42
),
we find that

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{M}_{2}|

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\epsilon_{1},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{1}{|v|^{2}|u|\log Q}}}\right)

\displaystyle\ll\frac{(\log Q)^{\epsilon}}{\log Q}\int_{(\frac{-3}{\log Q})}\int_{(-\epsilon_{1})}\frac{|\zeta(1-2v)|}{|u+v|^{A}|\zeta(1+u-v)||v|^{2}|u|}|du||dv|

\displaystyle\ll{(\log Q)^{\epsilon}}\int_{(\frac{-3}{\log Q})}\frac{1}{|v|^{2}}|dv|\ll(\log Q)^{1+\epsilon}

for any
A\geq 0
and
\epsilon>0
, where
\epsilon_{1}
needs to be sufficiently small depending on
\epsilon
.

For
t_{1}\geq 0
and
t_{2}\leq 0
,
we shift the
v
-contour in (
9.40
) to
\textup{Re}(v)=-\epsilon_{1}
and then change the order of integrals, we find that

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},-\epsilon_{1};\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}}}\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},-\epsilon_{1};\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}}}{v^{2}}\int_{-\infty}^{0}Q^{-vt_{2}}\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2}}}\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})dt_{1}.

After the
t_{2}
-integration by parts, we find that the above is

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{M}_{2}|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},-\epsilon_{1};\frac{1}{|v|^{3}\log Q}}}\right)

\displaystyle\ll

\displaystyle(\log Q)^{\epsilon}\int_{(-\epsilon_{1})}\int_{(\frac{-1}{\log Q})}\frac{1}{|u+v|^{A}|\zeta(1-u+v)||v|^{3}}|du||dv|\ll(\log Q)^{\epsilon}

for any
A\geq 0
and
\epsilon>0
by Lemma
2.19
and (
9.42
).
∎

Next, we compute the integral in Lemma
10.1
.

Lemma 10.2
.

Assume (
5.6
) and
|W_{1}|,|W_{2}|\leq W
for some
W>0
, then we have

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},2}(W_{1},W_{2})=(\log Q)^{2}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{8L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\mathscr{I}(\Phi_{K_{1}},\Phi_{K_{2}};W_{1}-1,W_{2}-1)+O((\log Q)^{1+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
, where
\mathscr{I}
is defined in (
1.12
).

Proof.

Let
t_{1},t_{2}\geq 0
. Then we shift the
u
-contour to
\textup{Re}(u)=\epsilon_{1}>0
and obtain

(10.4)

\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{-1}{\log Q},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}}}\right)=\mathfrak{R}_{1}+\mathfrak{R}_{2}+\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\epsilon_{1},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}}}\right),

where we recall that
\mathfrak{M}_{2}
is defined in (
9.40
),
\mathfrak{R}_{1}
is the residue at
u=0
from the factor
\zeta(1-2u)
, and
\mathfrak{R}_{2}
is the residue at
u=-v
from the factor
\zeta(1-u-v)\Gamma(-u-v)
.
The contribution of the last term in (
10.4
) to
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn},2}(W_{1},W_{2})
in Lemma
10.1
is

(10.5)

\begin{split}&\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{M}_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\epsilon_{1},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{Q^{-ut_{1}-vt_{2}}}{v^{2}}}}\right)\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}\\
&\ll|\mathfrak{M}_{2}|\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\epsilon_{1},\frac{-3}{\log Q};\frac{1}{|v|^{2}|u|\log Q}}}\right)\ll(\log Q)^{1+\epsilon}\end{split}

for any
\epsilon>0
, similarly to the proof of Lemma
10.1
.

Next we compute the residue
\mathfrak{R}_{1}
at
u=0
.
By (
2.19
) we find that

\mathfrak{R}_{1}=\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\frac{-3}{\log Q})}\widetilde{\Psi}(v+1)\mathfrak{J}_{3}(0,v;\mathbb{L})Q^{-vt_{2}}\frac{\zeta(-v)\zeta(1-2v)}{\zeta(1+v)}\frac{\Gamma(-v)\Gamma(v+\frac{k}{2})\cos\frac{\pi v}{2}}{v^{2}\Gamma(-v+\frac{k}{2})}dv.

Since

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{\infty}Q^{-vt_{2}}\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2}=&-\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime}_{K_{2}}(W_{2})-v\log Q\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{2}}(W_{2})\\
&+v^{2}(\log Q)^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}Q^{-vt_{2}}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2},\end{split}

we find that

\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{R}_{1}\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2}=(\log Q)^{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4\pi i}\int_{(\frac{-3}{\log Q})}\widetilde{\Psi}(v+1)\mathfrak{J}_{3}(0,v;\mathbb{L})Q^{-vt_{2}}\\
\times\frac{\zeta(-v)\zeta(1-2v)}{\zeta(1+v)}\frac{\Gamma(-v)\Gamma(v+\frac{k}{2})\cos\frac{\pi v}{2}}{\Gamma(-v+\frac{k}{2})}dv\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2}+O((\log Q)^{1+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
. By shifting the
v
-contour to
\textup{Re}(v)=\epsilon_{1}>0
, the residue at
v=0
is

(\log Q)^{2}\frac{1}{8}\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\mathfrak{J}_{3}(0,0;\mathbb{L})\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2}.

For the integral shifted to
\textup{Re}(v)=\epsilon_{1}
, we integrate by parts twice with respect to
v
and obtain that it is
O((\log Q)^{1+\epsilon})
for any
\epsilon>0
.
By Lemma
9.3
, (
9.16
) and (
9.41
), we find that

(10.6)

\mathfrak{J}_{3}(-v,v;\mathbb{L})=\frac{1}{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}.

By collecting the above estimations we find that

(10.7)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{R}_{1}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}\\
=(\log Q)^{2}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{8L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})dt_{1}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2}+O((\log Q)^{1+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
.

Lastly, we find that

\mathfrak{R}_{2}=\frac{-i^{-k}}{8\pi}\int_{(\frac{-3}{\log Q})}\oint_{|u|=\frac{1}{\log Q}}\widetilde{\Psi}(u+1)\mathfrak{J}_{3}(u-v,v)Q^{-ut_{1}+v(t_{1}-t_{2})}\\
\times\frac{\zeta(-u)\zeta(1-2v)\zeta(1-2u+2v)(\mathcal{G}_{u-2v,k-1}(u+1)-\mathcal{G}_{2v-u,k-1}(u+1))}{\zeta(1+u-2v)\zeta(1-u+2v)\sin(\frac{\pi}{2}(u-2v))}\frac{du}{u^{2}}\frac{dv}{v^{2}}.

We have a double pole at
u=0
.
By (
2.20
) and (
2.22
), we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\int_{(\frac{-3}{\log Q})}\oint_{|u|=\frac{1}{\log Q}}\mathfrak{H}(u,v)Q^{-ut_{1}+v(t_{1}-t_{2})}\frac{(u-2v)(-u+2v)}{(-2v)(-2u+2v)}\frac{du}{u^{2}}\frac{dv}{v^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\pi i}{2}\int_{(\frac{-3}{\log Q})}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-t_{1}\log Q\mathfrak{H}(0,v)+\frac{\partial\mathfrak{H}}{\partial u}(0,v)}}\right)Q^{v(t_{1}-t_{2})}\frac{dv}{v^{2}},

where

\mathfrak{H}(u,v):=\widetilde{\Psi}(u+1)\mathfrak{J}_{3}(u-v,v;\mathbb{L})\frac{\zeta(-u)\zeta(1-2v)\zeta(1-2u+2v)}{\zeta(1+u-2v)\zeta(1-u+2v)}\frac{\cos\frac{\pi u}{2}}{\sin(\pi u-\pi v)\sin(\pi v)}\\
\times\frac{(-2v)(-2u+2v)}{(u-2v)(-u+2v)\Gamma(-v-\frac{k}{2}+1)\Gamma(-u+v-\frac{k}{2}+1)\Gamma(\frac{k}{2}-u+v)\Gamma(\frac{k}{2}-v)}

is analytic at
u=0
and
v=0
, and
\mathfrak{H}(0,v)=-\frac{1}{2\pi^{2}}\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\frac{1}{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}
.
We shift the contour to
\textup{Re}(v)=\epsilon_{1}>0
if
t_{1}\leq t_{2}
and to
\textup{Re}(v)=-\epsilon_{1}
if
t_{1}>t_{2}
, then we find that

\mathfrak{R}_{2}=(\log Q)^{2}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{2L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\delta_{t_{1}\leq t_{2}}t_{1}(t_{1}-t_{2})+O((\log Q)^{1+\epsilon})

for any
\epsilon>0
.
Since

\int_{t_{1}}^{\infty}(t_{1}-t_{2})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{2}=-\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{2}}(t_{1}+W_{2}),

The contribution of
\mathfrak{R}_{2}
is

(10.8)

\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\mathfrak{R}_{2}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}^{\prime\prime}_{K_{2}}(t_{2}+W_{2})dt_{1}dt_{2}\\
=-(\log Q)^{2}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{2L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\int_{0}^{\infty}t_{1}\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{1}}(t_{1}+W_{1})\widehat{\Phi}_{K_{2}}(t_{1}+W_{2})dt_{1}+O((\log Q)^{1+\epsilon}).

The lemma follows from (
10.4
), (
10.5
), (
10.7
) and (
10.8
).
∎

10.1.
Proof of Proposition
8.2
– off-diagonal main terms.

By Lemmas
9.13
and
10.2
, and (
10.1
),

\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=Q(\log Q)^{|K|}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{8L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}=K\\
K_{1},K_{2}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}(-2)^{|K_{3}|+|K_{4}|}\\
\times\int_{[0,\infty)^{|K_{3}|+|K_{4}|}}\mathscr{I}\bigg(\Phi_{K_{1}},\Phi_{K_{2}};\sum_{k_{j}\in K_{3}}w_{k_{j}},\sum_{k_{j}\in K_{4}}w_{k_{j}}\bigg)\prod_{k_{j}\in K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}(w_{k_{j}})dw_{k_{j}}}}\right)\\
+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q(\log Q)^{|K|-1+\epsilon}}{L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty}}}}\right)

for any
\epsilon>0
. Since
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
are not empty, let
\ell_{1}
and
\ell_{2}
be their minimums, respectively. Then we replace
K_{1}=\{\ell_{1}\}\sqcup K_{1}^{\prime}
and
K_{2}=\{\ell_{2}\}\sqcup K_{2}^{\prime}
, and we see that
\Phi_{K_{1}}=\Phi_{\ell_{1},K_{1}^{\prime}}
and
\Phi_{K_{2}}=\Phi_{\ell_{2},K_{2}^{\prime}}
, where
\Phi_{\ell_{i},K_{i}^{\prime}}
is defined in (
1.13
). By (
1.11
), the main term of
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}
is

\displaystyle Q(\log Q)^{|K|}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{8L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\sum_{\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}=K}\frac{2}{(-2)^{|K|-2}}\mathscr{V}(\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\},K^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\ Q(\log Q)^{|K|}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(1)\delta_{\ell_{\infty}=1}}{(-2)^{|K|}L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=2\end{subarray}}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime}).

This proves the proposition.

11.
Lemma
5.3
– the term
\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},<}(Q)

Let
K=\{k_{1},\ldots,k_{\kappa}\}
and assume that
K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\subset K
and
K_{1}\neq\emptyset
. In this section, we prove Lemma
5.3
by induction on
|K_{2}|
. Recall the definitions in and below (
5.4
):

(11.1)

\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},<}(Q):=\frac{(-2)^{\kappa}}{(\log Q)^{\kappa}}\operatornamewithlimits{\sum\nolimits^{\prime}}_{\mathbb{L}}\frac{\mu(L_{1}L_{2})\zeta_{L_{1}}(2)}{L_{1}L_{2}}\frac{\mu(\ell_{1}\ell_{2})}{\ell_{1}^{2}\ell_{\infty}}C_{K_{1},K_{2},<}(Q;\mathbb{L}),

where the prime sum is over
\mathbb{L}
satisfying (
5.6
), and

(11.2)

\begin{split}C_{K_{1},K_{2},<}&(Q;\mathbb{L}):=\sum_{n}\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}{Q}}}\right)\\
&\times\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|n,\,\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),n)=1\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\Delta_{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}n}(\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty}^{2}).\end{split}

Since

\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|n,\,\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{2}),n)=1\end{subarray}}=\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|n,\,\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}=K_{2}\\
K_{3}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}\sqcup K_{3})|n,\,\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{4}),n)=1\end{subarray}},

we expect that

(11.3)

\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},K_{2},<}(Q)=\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},\emptyset,<}(Q)-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}=K_{2}\\
K_{3}\neq\emptyset\end{subarray}}\mathscr{C}_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{3},K_{4},<}(Q)+O\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{Q}{\log Q}}}\right).

The
O
-term is the contribution of the distinct primes
p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}
satisfying
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1
,
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}\sqcup K_{3})|n
,
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}\sqcup K_{3})\geq\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
and
(\mathfrak{p}(K_{4}),n)=1
for
K_{3}\sqcup K_{4}=K_{2}
and
K_{3}\neq\emptyset
.
If we replace
K_{1}
,
K_{1}\sqcup K_{3}
and
K_{4}
by
K_{1}^{\prime}
,
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
, respectively, with an additional condition
K_{1}^{\prime}\subset K_{1}
, then it is easy to see that (
11.3
) follows from the proof of Lemma
5.4
by adding a condition
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}^{\prime})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
appropriately. So we omit the details.

Since
|K_{4}|<|K_{2}|
in (
11.3
), we already have the inductive step (
11.3
) to prove Lemma
5.3
. Thus, to complete the proof, it is sufficient to show the initial case

(11.4)

\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},\emptyset,<}(Q)\ll\frac{Q}{\log Q}

for every nonempty
K_{1}\subset K
. We will sketch how to modify the arguments in §
6
–§
9
to prove (
11.4
).

First, we remove the condition
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|n
and replace
n
by
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n
in (
11.2
). Then we see that

(11.5)

\begin{split}C_{K_{1},\emptyset,<}&(Q;\mathbb{L})=\sum_{n}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\\
&\times\Psi\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{L_{1}^{2}L_{2}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n}{Q}}}\right)\Delta_{L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n}(\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty}^{2}).\end{split}

If we compare it with (
5.7
), we can obtain (
11.5
) by replacing
n
with
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n
and adding the condition
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
to (
5.7
).
Applying Petersson’s formula and following the arguments in the beginning of §
6
, we have

(11.6)

C_{K_{1},\emptyset,<}(Q;\mathbb{L})\\
=2\pi i^{-k}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c\geq 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}v_{j}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\times\sum_{n}\frac{S(\ell_{\infty}^{2},\mathfrak{p}(K);cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n)}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n}h_{u}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{4\pi\ell_{\infty}\sqrt{\mathfrak{p}(K)}}{cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n}}}\right)dudv_{1}\cdots dv_{\kappa}

similarly to (
6.7
).

We apply Kuznetsov’s formula to (
11.6
) as described in §
6
, but with
N=cL_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})
and
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
.
Arguing as in §
7
and §
8.1
, we obtain bounds analogous to Propositions
6.2
and
8.1
for the contribution from the discrete spectrum, the holomorphic forms, and the Eisenstein series associated to non-principal characters. We essentially need to replace
n
by
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})n
and add conditions
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
to the sums over the primes in §
6
, §
7
and §

. Since the bounds in Propositions

and

have a factor
Q^{\epsilon}
with arbitrary
