, we see that
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
is small enough so that we may crudely estimate the sums over
p_{k_{j}}
for
k_{j}\in K_{1}
.

By Kuznetsov’s formula to (
11.6
) as in the previous paragraph, we find that the contribution from the Eisenstein series associated to the principal characters is

\Sigma^{\prime}_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}:=\frac{i^{-k}}{2}\sideset{}{{}^{d}}{\sum}_{P_{1},\ldots,P_{\kappa}}\sum_{c\geq 1}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\cdots\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\sqrt{p_{k_{j}}}}V\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}}}\right)\operatorname{e}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{p_{k_{j}}}{P_{j}}v_{j}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\times\sum_{M|cL^{\prime}_{0}}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL^{\prime}_{0}}(\mathfrak{p}(K),t)\overline{\rho_{\chi_{0},M,cL^{\prime}_{0}}(\ell_{\infty}^{2},t)}h_{u,+}(t)\>dt\widehat{H}(u,\bm{v})dudv_{1}\cdots dv_{\kappa}

with
L^{\prime}_{0}=L_{0}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})=L_{1}\ell_{1}\ell_{2}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})
. This equals to
\Sigma_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}
in (
9.1
) except for
L^{\prime}_{0}
and the condition
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}
.
By following the arguments in §
9
, we find that

(11.7)

\Sigma^{\prime}_{\textup{Ctn}_{0}}=\frac{i^{-k}}{2}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\int_{0}^{\infty}\int_{(-\epsilon_{1})}\frac{\widetilde{\Psi}(s)Q^{s}}{(4\pi L_{1}L_{2}\ell_{\infty})^{s}}\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{k_{1}},\ldots,p_{k_{\kappa}}\\
(\mathfrak{p}(K),L_{1}L_{2})=1\\
\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})<\mathcal{L}_{3\kappa}\end{subarray}}\prod_{j=1}^{\kappa}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{p_{k_{j}}^{\frac{1}{2}(1+s)}}\widehat{\Phi}_{k_{j}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\log p_{k_{j}}}{\log Q}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\times\widetilde{\varrho}_{L^{\prime}_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(-s)(J_{2it}(\xi)-J_{-2it}(\xi))J_{k-1}(\xi)\xi^{s-1}dsd\xi\frac{dt}{\sinh(\pi t)}

similarly to (
9.5
).

Next, we find an expression for
\widetilde{\varrho}_{L^{\prime}_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s)
.
By Lemma
9.1
with
L^{\prime}_{0}
in place of
L_{0}
, we have

\widetilde{\varrho}_{L^{\prime}_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s)=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)^{it}}{L_{0}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\ell_{\infty}^{2it}|\zeta(1+2it)|^{2}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}d_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)\\
d_{2}|\ell_{\infty}^{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\mu(d_{1}d_{2})}{d_{1}^{2it}d_{2}^{-2it}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}c_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)/d_{1}\\
c_{2}|\ell_{\infty}^{2}/d_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{c_{2}^{2it}}{c_{1}^{2it}}F_{L^{\prime}_{0},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it),

where
\mathfrak{m}=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)\ell_{\infty}^{2}}{c_{1}c_{2}d_{1}d_{2}}
. By applying Lemma
9.2
with

\alpha=L_{0}^{\prime}=L_{0}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})
,
r=d_{1}d_{2}
,
\alpha_{1}:=\frac{L_{0}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})}{(L_{0}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}),d_{1}d_{2})}=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})}{(\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}),d_{1})}\cdot\frac{L_{0}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})}
and
r_{1}:=\frac{d_{1}d_{2}}{(L_{0}\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}),d_{1}d_{2})}=\frac{d_{1}}{(d_{1},\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}))}\cdot\frac{d_{2}}{(d_{2},\ell_{2})}
as in the beginning of §
9.2
, we find that

(11.8)

F_{L_{0}^{\prime},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=F_{L_{0},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)(d_{1},\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}))^{1+s}\\
\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\\
p\nmid d_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}+\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{s+1}(p-1)}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{m}_{1}\\
p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})/(d_{1},\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}))\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)^{-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\\
p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{1}d_{1}\end{subarray}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}

with
\mathfrak{m}_{1}=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)}{c_{1}d_{1}}
.
By (
9.15
) and (
11.8
), we have

F_{L_{0}^{\prime},d_{1}d_{2},\mathfrak{m}}(s,it)=\zeta(1+s)\widetilde{F}(s,it)\mathfrak{J}_{1}(s,it;\mathbb{L},c_{2},d_{2})\frac{(d_{1},\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}))^{1+s}}{d_{1}^{1+s}}\\
\prod_{p|d_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|\mathfrak{m}_{1}d_{1}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}\prod_{p|\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\\
\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\\
p\nmid d_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}+\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{s+1}(p-1)}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\\
p|\mathfrak{m}_{1},\penalty\ p\nmid d_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)^{-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\\
p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{1}d_{1}\end{subarray}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}.

Thus, we find that

\widetilde{\varrho}_{L^{\prime}_{0},\mathfrak{p}(K),\ell_{\infty};t}(s)=\frac{\mathfrak{p}(K)^{it}\zeta(1+s)\widetilde{F}(s,it)\mathfrak{J}_{2}(s,it;\mathbb{L})}{\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})|\zeta(1+2it)|^{2}}\sum_{c_{1}d_{1}|\mathfrak{p}(K)}\frac{\mu(d_{1})(d_{1},\mathfrak{p}(K_{1}))^{1+s}}{c_{1}^{2it}d_{1}^{1+s+2it}}\\
\prod_{p|d_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{1+s}(p-1)}}}\right)\prod_{p|\mathfrak{m}_{1}d_{1}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}\prod_{p|\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)\\
\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\\
p\nmid d_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{p}-\frac{1}{p^{2+s}}+\delta_{p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\frac{1}{p^{s+1}(p-1)}}}\right)}}\right)\\
\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\\
p|\mathfrak{m}_{1},\penalty\ p\nmid d_{1}\end{subarray}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(W_{p}(s,it)-\frac{1}{p^{1+s}}-\frac{1}{p^{2+2s}(p-1)}}}\right)^{-1}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}p|\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})\\
p\nmid\mathfrak{m}_{1}d_{1}\end{subarray}}W_{p}(s,it)^{-1}.

Due to the factor
\frac{1}{\mathfrak{p}(K_{1})}
in the first line of the above display, when we follow the arguments in §
9
, we expect that the sum over the primes
p_{k_{j}}
for
k_{j}\in K_{1}
is
(\log Q)^{|K_{1}|}
smaller so that

\mathscr{C}_{K_{1},\emptyset,<}(Q)\ll\frac{Q}{(\log Q)^{|K_{1}|}}

would hold. This implies (
11.4
).

12.
n
-th centered moments for
O(N)

In this section we prove Theorem
1.2
. Recall that
SO(2N)
and
USp(2N)
are the classical even orthogonal and symplectic groups, respectively.
Define

(12.1)

\begin{split}\mathscr{T}_{+}(S):=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathscr{T}_{+,N}(S)&:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{SO(2N)}\bigg(\prod_{\ell\in S}\sum_{0<|j|\leq N}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N\theta_{j}}{\pi}}}\right)\bigg)dX_{SO(2N)},\end{split}

(12.2)

\begin{split}\mathscr{T}_{-}(S):=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathscr{T}_{-,N}(S)&:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{USp(2N)}\bigg(\prod_{\ell\in S}\sum_{0<|j|\leq N}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N\theta_{j}}{\pi}}}\right)\bigg)dX_{USp(2N)}\end{split}

for
S\subset\{1,\ldots,n\}
. By Lemma
12.4
we have

(12.3)

\mathscr{T}_{-}(S)=\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{O^{-}(2N+2)}\bigg(\prod_{\ell\in S}\sum_{0<|j|\leq N}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N\theta_{j}}{\pi}}}\right)\bigg)dX_{O^{-}(2N+2)}.

Now, by (
1.4
), (
1.5
), (
12.1
) and (
12.3
), we have

(12.4)

\begin{split}C_{even}(n)&=\sum_{S_{1}\sqcup S_{2}=[n]}\mathscr{T}_{+}(S_{1})\prod_{\ell\in S_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(0)-\frac{\Phi_{\ell}(0)}{2}}}\right),\\
C_{odd}(n)&=\sum_{S_{1}\sqcup S_{2}=[n]}\mathscr{T}_{-}(S_{1})\prod_{\ell\in S_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(-\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(0)+\frac{\Phi_{\ell}(0)}{2}}}\right).\end{split}

This reduces our problem to computing the limits

\mathscr{T}_{\pm}:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathscr{T}_{\pm,N}:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\mathscr{T}_{\pm,N}([\nu]),

where without loss of generality, we have replaced
S
by
[\nu]
when
S\subset[n]
with
|S|=\nu
,
1\leq\nu\leq n
.

First we apply results from Mason and Snaith
[
32
]
to (
12.1
) and (
12.2
). The work of Mason and Snaith builds upon the method introduced by Conrey and Snaith
[
12
]
, who derived a new formula for
n
-correlations of eigenvalues of matrices from the unitary group and subsequently applied it in
[
13
]
to match number theory results with random matrix predictions. Although their formula appears intricate, it expresses the result in terms of a test function whose Fourier transform may have restricted support. This formulation allows one to identify immediately which terms survive under the support restrictions on the test function, thereby aligning with the number theoretic calculations.

For notational convenience we rename the functions
J^{*}
and
J_{USp(2N)}^{*}
defined at (2.26) and (3.12) in
[
32
]
as
J_{+}^{*}
and
J_{-}^{*}
, respectively, and define

(12.5)

J_{\pm}^{*}(W,N):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}W^{\prime}\sqcup W^{\prime\prime}\sqcup W_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup W_{R}=W\\
|W_{r}|=2\end{subarray}}e^{-2N\sum_{w\in W^{\prime}}w}H_{0}^{\mp}(W^{\prime})\prod_{\alpha\in W^{\prime\prime}}H_{1}^{\mp}(W^{\prime},\alpha)\prod_{r=1}^{R}H_{2}(W_{r}),

where

(12.6)

\begin{split}H_{0}^{\mp}(W^{\prime})&:=(-1)^{|W^{\prime}|}\prod_{\{\alpha,\beta\}\subset W^{\prime},\alpha\neq\beta}\frac{(1-e^{-\alpha+\beta})(1-e^{\alpha-\beta})}{(1-e^{-\alpha-\beta})(1-e^{\alpha+\beta})}\prod_{\alpha\in W^{\prime}}\frac{1}{1-e^{\mp 2\alpha}},\\
H_{1}^{\mp}(W^{\prime},\alpha)&:=\sum_{w\in W^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{1-e^{\alpha-w}}-\frac{1}{1-e^{\alpha+w}}}}\right)\mp\frac{1}{1-e^{2\alpha}},\\
H_{2}(\{\alpha,\beta\})&:=\frac{e^{\alpha+\beta}}{(1-e^{\alpha+\beta})^{2}}.\end{split}

Here,
W^{\prime}
corresponds to
D
in the definitions of
J^{*}
and
J_{USp(2N)}^{*}
in
[
32
]
,
W^{\prime\prime}
is the union of the
W_{r}
with
|W_{r}|=1
and
R
is any positive integer.

We further fix, for
1\leq\ell\leq\nu
, positive numbers
\delta_{\ell}
to be determined later. Let

(12.7)

\delta(\ell,N):=\frac{\log\log N}{N}\delta_{\ell}.

For clarity, we record the following lemma.

Lemma 12.1
.

Fix notation as above and suppose that
z_{\ell}=u\pm it
with
|u|\leq\delta(\ell,N)
and the support of
\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}
is contained in
[-\sigma_{\ell},\sigma_{\ell}].

Then

(12.8)

\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)\ll_{A}\frac{(\log N)^{2\sigma_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}}}{(N|z_{\ell}|)^{A}},

for any integer
A\geq 0
.

Proof.

Our condition on
z_{\ell}
implies that
|e^{2Nz_{\ell}x}|\leq e^{2N\sigma_{\ell}\delta(\ell,N)}\leq(\log N)^{2\sigma_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}}
, for
\delta(\ell,N)
as in (
12.7
). Thus, by integration by parts
A
times, we have

(12.9)

\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(x)e^{-2Nz_{\ell}x}dx\ll_{A}\frac{(\log N)^{2\sigma_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}}}{(N|z_{\ell}|)^{A}},

as desired.
∎

Lemma 12.2
.

Let notation be as above. Then we have

(12.10)

\mathscr{T}_{\pm}=\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{K\sqcup\tilde{K}=[\nu]}\frac{N^{|\tilde{K}|}}{(\pi i)^{\nu}}\int_{(\delta(\ell,N);[\nu])}J_{\pm}^{*}(z_{K},N)\prod_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)dz_{1}\cdots dz_{\nu},

where

(\delta(\ell,N);L)
means that the
z_{\ell}
-integral for each
\ell\in L
is over the vertical line from
\delta(\ell,N)-i\infty
to
\delta(\ell,N)+i\infty
, and

z_{K}:=\{z_{k}:k\in K\}.

Proof.

Let

C_{\pm}
be the path from
\pm\frac{\log\log N}{N}\delta-\pi i
to
\pm\frac{\log\log N}{N}\delta+\pi i
for a small
\delta>0
.
By applying
[
32
, Lemma 2.9]
to (
12.1
), we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{T}_{+,N}=

\displaystyle\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=[\nu]}\frac{(2N)^{|K_{3}|}}{(2\pi i)^{\nu}}\int_{C_{+}^{K_{1}}}\int_{C_{-}^{K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}}}J_{+}^{*}(z_{K_{1}}\sqcup-z_{K_{2}},N)\prod_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)dz_{1}\cdots dz_{\nu}

\displaystyle+o(1)

as
N\to\infty
, where
\int_{C_{+}^{K_{1}}}\int_{C_{-}^{K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}}}
means we are integrating all the variables in
z_{K_{1}}
along the
C_{+}
path and all others up to the
C_{-}
path and
-z_{K}:=\{-z_{k}:k\in K\}
. In our application of
[
32
, Lemma 2.9]
, we have taken their
f
to be

f(\theta_{j_{1}},\ldots,\theta_{j_{\nu}})=\prod_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}}}\right).

In
[
32
, Lemma 2.9]
,
f
is assumed to be periodic, which is used in the proof in order to show that certain horizontal integrals cancel out. Those same horizontal integrals can be justified to be
o(1)
in our case by the bound from Lemma
12.1
, which implies that

(12.11)

\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)\ll_{A}\frac{(\log N)^{2\sigma_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}}}{(N|z_{\ell}|)^{A}}\ll_{A}\frac{(\log N)^{2\sigma_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}}}{N^{A}},

for any integer
A\geq 0
, since
|z_{\ell}|\geq\pi
.

Now, since the integrand is holomorphic in
z_{k}
for all
z_{k}\in K_{3}
, we may shift each
z_{k}
contour for
k\in K_{3}
to
C^{+}
, where again the horizontal parts are small by (
12.8
) from Lemma
12.1
. We further substitute
z_{k}
by
-z_{k}
for each
k\in K_{2}
to see that

(12.12)

\mathscr{T}_{+,N}=\sum_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}\sqcup K_{3}=[\nu]}\frac{(2N)^{|K_{3}|}}{(2\pi i)^{\nu}}\int_{C_{+}^{[\nu]}}J_{+}^{*}(z_{K_{1}\sqcup K_{2}},N)\prod_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)dz_{1}\cdots dz_{\nu}+o(1),

where we have used the fact that
\Phi_{\ell}
is even for all
\ell
. Since the integrand in (
12.12
) is holomorphic, we shift each
z_{\ell}
contour in (
12.12
) to the line segment from
\delta(\ell,N)-\pi i
to
\delta(\ell,N)+\pi i
. By the shifts, we obtain extra terms containing horizontal
z_{\ell}
-integrals, which are also negligible by (
12.8
).
We can also extend these integrals to the vertical line from
\delta(\ell,N)-i\infty
to
\delta(\ell,N)+i\infty
in a similar way. By combining the sum over
K_{1}
and
K_{2}
as a sum over
K
and replacing
K_{3}
by
\tilde{K}
, we prove the even case.

To prove the odd case, we apply
[
32
, Lemma 3.5]
instead and argue similarly to the even case.
∎

We integrate the
z_{\ell}
-integrals in (
12.10
) for
\ell\in\tilde{K}
.
For each
\ell\in\tilde{K}
, we see that

\frac{N}{\pi i}\int_{(\delta(\ell,N))}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)dz_{\ell}=\frac{N}{\pi i}\int_{-i\infty}^{i\infty}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)dz_{\ell}=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\Phi_{\ell}(z_{\ell})dz_{\ell}=\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(0).

Thus, we find that

(12.13)

\mathscr{T}_{\pm}=\sum_{K\sqcup\tilde{K}=[\nu]}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{\ell\in\tilde{K}}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(0)}}\right)\mathscr{U}_{\pm}(K),

where

(12.14)

\mathscr{U}_{\pm}(K):=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{(\pi i)^{|K|}}\int_{(\delta(\ell,N);K)}J_{\pm}^{*}(z_{K},N)\prod_{\ell\in K}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)dz_{\ell}.

Define

(12.15)

\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K):=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{(\pi i)^{|K|}}\int_{(\delta(\ell,N);K)}J_{\pm,j}^{*}(z_{K},N)\prod_{\ell\in K}\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)dz_{\ell}

where

(12.16)

J_{\pm,j}^{*}(z_{K},N):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup K_{R}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=j,\penalty\ |K_{r}|=2\end{subarray}}e^{-2N\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}z_{k}}H_{0}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}})\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime\prime}}H_{1}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}},z_{\ell})\prod_{r=1}^{R}H_{2}(z_{K_{r}})

for
j\geq 0
, then we see that

(12.17)

\mathscr{U}_{\pm}(K)=\sum_{j=0}^{|K|}\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K).

Due to the support conditions on
\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}
, the above sum is actually shorter.

Lemma 12.3
.

Assume that the support of
\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}
is contained in
[-\sigma_{\ell},\sigma_{\ell}]
for
\ell\leq\nu
and

\sum_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\sigma_{\ell}<4
. Then

(12.18)

\mathscr{U}_{\pm}(K)=\sum_{j=0}^{3}\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K).

Proof.

By (
12.17
) it is enough to show that
\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K)=0
for
j\geq 4
.
Let
\epsilon_{1}:=4-\sum_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\sigma_{\ell}>0
.
We choose
\delta_{1},\ldots,\delta_{\nu}
satisfying

0<\delta_{1}<\cdots<\delta_{\nu}\leq\frac{8-\epsilon_{1}}{8-2\epsilon_{1}}\delta_{1}.

For notational convenience, let
\lambda_{N}=\frac{\log\log N}{N},
and note that
\textup{Re}z_{k}\geq\delta_{1}\lambda_{N}
, so that
e^{-Nz_{k}}\ll(\log N)^{-\delta_{1}}
.
Putting this into (
12.16
), we get

\displaystyle|J_{\pm,j}^{*}(z_{K},N)|

\displaystyle\ll(\log N)^{-2j\delta_{1}}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{1}\sqcup\cdots\sqcup K_{R}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=j,\penalty\ |K_{r}|=2\end{subarray}}|H_{0}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}})|\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime\prime}}|H_{1}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}},z_{\ell})|\prod_{r=1}^{R}|H_{2}(z_{K_{r}})|

\displaystyle\ll(\log N)^{-2j\delta_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N}{\log\log N}}}\right)^{|K|-j}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\subset K\\
|K^{\prime}|=j\end{subarray}}|H_{0}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}})|,

where we have used the crude bounds
|H_{1}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}},z_{\ell})|\ll\frac{N}{\log\log N}
, and
|H_{2}(z_{K_{r}})|\ll\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N}{\log\log N}}}\right)^{2}
.
By Lemma
12.1
, we see that

\int_{(\delta(\ell,N))}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)}}\right||dz_{\ell}|\ll\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{(\log N)^{2\sigma_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}}}{(N|\delta(\ell,N)+it|)^{2}}dt\leq\frac{(\log N)^{\sigma_{\ell}\frac{8-\epsilon_{1}}{4-\epsilon_{1}}\delta_{1}}}{N\log\log N}

for
\ell\in K\setminus K^{\prime}
.
Hence, by applying the above inequalities to (
12.15
), we find that

\displaystyle\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K)

\displaystyle\ll\lim_{N\to\infty}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\subset K\\
|K^{\prime}|=j\end{subarray}}(\log N)^{-2j\delta_{1}}\prod_{\ell\in K\backslash K^{\prime}}\frac{(\log N)^{\sigma_{\ell}\frac{8-\epsilon_{1}}{4-\epsilon_{1}}\delta_{1}}}{(\log\log N)^{2}}

(12.19)

\displaystyle\times\int_{(\delta(\ell,N);K^{\prime})}|H_{0}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}})|\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)dz_{\ell}}}\right|.

We now consider two cases of the
z_{\ell}
for
\ell\in K^{\prime}
in (
12.19
). When
|z_{\ell_{0}}|\geq\frac{1}{10}
for some
\ell_{0}\in K^{\prime}
, we use the crude bound

|H_{0}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}})|\ll\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{N}{\log\log N}}}\right)^{j+2\binom{j}{2}},

and the bound from Lemma
12.1
,

\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)\ll\frac{N^{\epsilon_{1}}}{(|z_{\ell}|N)^{A}},

for any
A>0
, noting that the above also implies that the integral over
z_{\ell_{0}}
satisfying
|z_{\ell_{0}}|\geq\frac{1}{10}
is
\ll\frac{1}{N^{A}}
for any
A>0
. Thus, the final contribution of this case of the
z_{\ell}
to
\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K)
in (
12.19
) is
\ll\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}N^{-A}=0
.

For
|z_{\ell}|\leq\frac{1}{10}
for all
\ell\in K^{\prime}
, we write
z_{\ell}=\delta_{\ell}\lambda_{N}+it_{\ell}
, and get

\displaystyle|H_{0}^{\mp}(z_{K^{\prime}})|

\displaystyle\ll\prod_{\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}\subset K^{\prime},\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}}\frac{(1-e^{-z_{\ell_{1}}+z_{\ell_{2}}})(1-e^{z_{\ell_{1}}-z_{\ell_{2}}})}{(1-e^{-z_{\ell_{1}}-z_{\ell_{2}}})(1-e^{z_{\ell_{1}}+z_{\ell_{2}}})}\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}}\frac{1}{1-e^{\mp 2z_{\ell}}}

\displaystyle\asymp\prod_{\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}\subset K^{\prime},\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}}\frac{(\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell_{1}}-t_{\ell_{2}}|)^{2}}{(\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell_{1}}+t_{\ell_{2}}|)^{2}}\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell}|}

\displaystyle=\prod_{\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}\subset K^{\prime},\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}}\frac{(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{t_{\ell_{1}}}{\lambda_{N}}-\frac{t_{\ell_{2}}}{\lambda_{N}}}}\right|)^{2}}{(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{t_{\ell_{1}}}{\lambda_{N}}+\frac{t_{\ell_{2}}}{\lambda_{N}}}}\right|)^{2}}\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell}|}

\displaystyle\leq\prod_{\{\ell_{1},\ell_{2}\}\subset K^{\prime},\ell_{1}\neq\ell_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{t_{\ell_{1}}}{\lambda_{N}}}}\right|}}\right)^{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{t_{\ell_{2}}}{\lambda_{N}}}}\right|}}\right)^{2}\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell}|}

\displaystyle=\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{t_{\ell}}{\lambda_{N}}}}\right|}}\right)^{2(j-1)}}{\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell}|}

Again by Lemma
12.1
,

\displaystyle\int_{(\delta(\ell,N))}

\displaystyle\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\Phi_{\ell}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{iNz_{\ell}}{\pi}}}\right)}}\right|\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{t_{\ell}}{\lambda_{N}}}}\right|}}\right)^{2(j-1)}}{\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell}|}|dz_{\ell}|

\displaystyle\ll\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\frac{(\log N)^{2\sigma_{\ell}\delta_{\ell}}}{(N(\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell}|))^{2+2(j-1)}}\frac{\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(1+\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left|\frac{t_{\ell}}{\lambda_{N}}}}\right|}}\right)^{2(j-1)}}{\lambda_{N}+|t_{\ell}|}dt_{\ell}\ll\frac{(\log N)^{\sigma_{\ell}\frac{8-\epsilon_{1}}{4-\epsilon_{1}}\delta_{1}}}{(\log\log N)^{2j}}.

Hence, by applying the above inequalities to (
12.19
) and ignoring negative powers of
\log\log N
, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K)

\displaystyle\ll\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(\log N)^{-2j\delta_{1}+\sum_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\sigma_{\ell}\frac{8-\epsilon_{1}}{4-\epsilon_{1}}\delta_{1}}\leq\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}(\log N)^{-\epsilon_{1}\delta_{1}}=0

for
j\geq 4
.
This proves the lemma.
∎

Next we compute
\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K)
.
By shifting each contour
(\delta(\ell,N))
to
(\pi\delta_{\ell}/N)
and then substituting
z_{\ell}=\pi w_{\ell}/N
, we find that

(12.20)

\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K)=\frac{1}{(\pi i)^{|K|}}\int_{(\delta_{\ell};K)}J_{\pm,j}^{**}(w_{K})\prod_{\ell\in K}\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{\ell})dw_{\ell},

where

J_{\pm,j}^{**}(w_{K}):=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\pi^{|K|}}{N^{|K|}}J_{\pm,j}^{*}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\pi}{N}w_{K},N}}\right).

Then by changing the order of the limit and the integrals we find that

J_{\pm,j}^{**}(w_{K})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{0}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=j\end{subarray}}\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K_{0},2}}e^{-2\pi\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}w_{k}}\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\mp}(w_{K^{\prime}})\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\mp}(w_{K^{\prime}},w_{\ell})\prod_{G_{i}\in\underline{G}}\mathcal{H}_{2}(w_{G_{i}}),

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\mp}(w_{K^{\prime}})

\displaystyle:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\pi^{|K^{\prime}|}}{N^{|K^{\prime}|}}H_{0}^{\mp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\pi}{N}w_{K^{\prime}}}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\mp}(w_{K^{\prime}},w_{\ell})

\displaystyle:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\pi}{N}H_{1}^{\mp}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\pi}{N}w_{K^{\prime}},\frac{\pi}{N}w_{\ell}}}\right),

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{2}(w_{G_{i}})

\displaystyle:=\lim_{N\to\infty}\frac{\pi^{2}}{N^{2}}H_{2}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{\pi}{N}w_{G_{i}}}}\right).

By (
12.6
) we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{0}^{\mp}(w_{K^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=(\mp 1)^{|K^{\prime}|}\mathcal{H}_{0}(w_{K^{\prime}}):=(\mp 1)^{|K^{\prime}|}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\in K^{\prime}\\
k_{1}>k_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{(w_{k_{1}}-w_{k_{2}})^{2}}{(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})^{2}}\prod_{k\in K^{\prime}}\frac{1}{2w_{k}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\mp}(w_{K^{\prime}},w_{\ell})

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\frac{1}{w_{\ell}+w_{k}}-\frac{1}{w_{\ell}-w_{k}}}}\right)\pm\frac{1}{2w_{\ell}},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{2}(\{w_{k_{1}},w_{k_{2}}\})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})^{2}}.

We first integrate the
w_{G_{i}}
integrals in (
12.20
). By Lemma
12.5
and (
1.10
) we have

\frac{1}{(\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{k})}\int_{(\delta_{m})}\frac{1}{(w_{m}+w_{k})^{2}}\Phi_{m}(iw_{m})\Phi_{k}(iw_{k})dw_{m}dw_{k}=\mathscr{I}_{2}(\{m,k\}).

Then we have

(12.21)

\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{0}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=j\end{subarray}}C_{0}(K_{0})\frac{(\mp 1)^{|K^{\prime}|}}{(\pi i)^{|K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}|}}\\
\times\int_{(\delta_{\ell};K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime})}e^{-2\pi\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}w_{k}}\mathcal{H}_{0}(w_{K^{\prime}})\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\mp}(w_{K^{\prime}},w_{\ell})\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}}\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{\ell})dw_{\ell},

where

(12.22)

C_{0}(K_{0}):=\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi_{K_{0},2}}\prod_{G_{i}\in\underline{G}}\mathscr{I}_{2}(G_{i}).

Next, for
\ell\in K^{\prime\prime}
we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\pi i}\int_{(\delta_{\ell})}

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\mp}(w_{K^{\prime}},w_{\ell})\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{\ell})dw_{\ell}=\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}4\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(t)e^{-2\pi tw_{k}}dt-\sum_{k\in K^{\prime},k<\ell}2\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{k})\pm\frac{\Phi_{\ell}(0)}{2}

by Lemma
12.5
. Thus, we have

(12.23)

\begin{split}&\mathscr{U}_{\pm,j}(K)\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{0}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=j\end{subarray}}C_{0}(K_{0})\frac{(\mp 1)^{|K^{\prime}|}}{(\pi i)^{|K^{\prime}|}}\int_{(\delta_{\ell};K^{\prime})}e^{-2\pi\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}w_{k}}\mathcal{H}_{0}(w_{K^{\prime}})\\
&\times\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}4\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(t)e^{-2\pi tw_{k}}dt-\sum_{k\in K^{\prime},k<\ell}2\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{k})\pm\frac{\Phi_{\ell}(0)}{2}}}\right)\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}}\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{\ell})dw_{\ell}\\
&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{0}=K\\
|K^{\prime}|=j\end{subarray}}C_{0}(K_{0})\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime\prime\prime}}\pm\frac{\Phi_{\ell}(0)}{2}}}\right)(\mp 1)^{|K^{\prime}|}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime})\end{split}

for
j\geq 0
, where
\mathscr{V}(\emptyset,\emptyset)=1
,
\mathscr{V}(\emptyset,K^{\prime\prime})=0
for
K^{\prime\prime}\neq\emptyset
and

(12.24)

\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime}):=\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{|K^{\prime}|}}\int_{(\delta_{\ell};K^{\prime})}e^{-2\pi\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}w_{k}}\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}k_{1},k_{2}\in K^{\prime}\\
k_{1}>k_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{(w_{k_{1}}-w_{k_{2}})^{2}}{(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})^{2}}\\
\times\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime\prime}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{k\in K^{\prime}}4\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(t)e^{-2\pi tw_{k}}dt-\sum_{k\in K^{\prime},k<\ell}2\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{k})}}\right)\prod_{\ell\in K^{\prime}}\frac{\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{\ell})}{w_{\ell}}dw_{\ell}

for
K^{\prime}\neq\emptyset
.

By (
12.13
), (
12.18
) and (
12.23
), we find that

(12.25)

\mathscr{T}_{\pm}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup\tilde{K}\sqcup K_{0}=[\nu]\\
|K^{\prime}|\leq 3\end{subarray}}C_{0}(K_{0})\prod_{\ell\in\tilde{K}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(0)\pm\frac{\Phi_{\ell}(0)}{2}}}\right)(\mp 1)^{|K^{\prime}|}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime}).

By (
12.4
) and (
12.25
), we find that

(12.26)

\begin{split}C_{even}(n)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{0}=[n]\\
|K^{\prime}|\leq 3\end{subarray}}C_{0}(K_{0})(-1)^{|K^{\prime}|}\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime})\\
C_{odd}(n)&=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{0}=[n]\\
|K^{\prime}|\leq 3\end{subarray}}C_{0}(K_{0})\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime}).\end{split}

By (
1.6
) and the above, the
n
-th centered moment for
O(N)
is

(12.27)

C(n)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}K^{\prime}\sqcup K^{\prime\prime}\sqcup K_{0}=[n]\\
|K^{\prime}|=0,2\end{subarray}}C_{0}(K_{0})\mathscr{V}(K^{\prime},K^{\prime\prime}).

By letting
C_{j}(n)
the contribution of the
K^{\prime}
with
|K^{\prime}|=j
, one can easily deduce the first part of Theorem
1.2
.

To complete the proof of Theorem
1.2
, it remains to compute
\mathscr{V}(\{k_{1},k_{2}\},G)
for
\{k_{1},k_{2}\}\sqcup G\subset[n]
. By (
12.24
), we see that

\begin{split}\mathscr{V}(\{k_{1},k_{2}\},G)=&\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{k_{1}})}\int_{(\delta_{k_{2}})}e^{-2\pi(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})}\\
&\times\prod_{\ell\in G}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\sum_{j=1,2}\bigg(4\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(t)e^{-2\pi tw_{k_{j}}}dt-2\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{k_{j}}){\bf 1}_{k_{j}<\ell}\bigg)}}\right)\\
&\times\frac{(w_{k_{1}}-w_{k_{2}})^{2}}{(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})^{2}}\frac{\Phi_{k_{1}}(iw_{k_{1}})\Phi_{k_{2}}(iw_{k_{2}})}{w_{k_{1}}w_{k_{2}}}dw_{k_{2}}dw_{k_{1}}.\end{split}

By expanding the product over
\ell\in G
we find that

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}

\displaystyle(\{k_{1},k_{2}\},G)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{G_{1}\sqcup G_{2}\sqcup G_{3}\sqcup G_{4}=G}4^{|G_{1}|+|G_{2}|}(-2)^{|G_{3}|+|G_{4}|}\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{k_{1}})}\int_{(\delta_{k_{2}})}e^{-2\pi(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\ell\in G_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(t)e^{-2\pi tw_{k_{1}}}dt}}\right)\prod_{\ell\in G_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(t)e^{-2\pi tw_{k_{2}}}dt}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\ell\in G_{3}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{k_{1}}){\bf 1}_{k_{1}<\ell}}}\right)\prod_{\ell\in G_{4}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{k_{2}}){\bf 1}_{k_{2}<\ell}}}\right)\frac{(w_{k_{1}}-w_{k_{2}})^{2}}{(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})^{2}}\frac{\Phi_{k_{1}}(iw_{k_{1}})\Phi_{k_{2}}(iw_{k_{2}})}{w_{k_{1}}w_{k_{2}}}dw_{k_{2}}dw_{k_{1}}.

If every element of
G_{3}
is bigger than
k_{1}
, then

\prod_{\ell\in G_{3}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\Phi_{\ell}(iw_{k_{1}}){\bf 1}_{k_{1}<\ell}}}\right)\Phi_{k_{1}}(iw_{k_{1}})=\Phi_{k_{1},G_{3}}(iw_{k_{1}}),

and equals
0
otherwise. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{V}(\{k_{1},k_{2}\},G)=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}G_{1}\sqcup G_{2}\sqcup G_{3}\sqcup G_{4}=G\\
G_{3}\subset\{k_{1}+1,\ldots,n\}\\
G_{4}\subset\{k_{2}+1,\ldots,n\}\end{subarray}}4^{|G_{1}|+|G_{2}|}(-2)^{|G_{3}|+|G_{4}|}\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{k_{1}})}\int_{(\delta_{k_{2}})}e^{-2\pi(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})}

\displaystyle\times\prod_{\ell\in G_{1}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(u_{\ell})e^{-2\pi u_{\ell}w_{k_{1}}}du_{\ell}}}\right)\prod_{\ell\in G_{2}}\mathopen{}\mathclose{{\left(\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{\ell}(u_{\ell})e^{-2\pi u_{\ell}w_{k_{2}}}du_{\ell}}}\right)

\displaystyle\times\frac{(w_{k_{1}}-w_{k_{2}})^{2}}{(w_{k_{1}}+w_{k_{2}})^{2}}\frac{\Phi_{k_{1},G_{3}}(iw_{k_{1}})\Phi_{k_{2},G_{4}}(iw_{k_{2}})}{w_{k_{1}}w_{k_{2}}}dw_{k_{2}}dw_{k_{1}}.

Now change the order of integration so that the
w_{k_{1}},w_{k_{2}}
are the innermost integrals. By Lemma
12.6
applied to the
w_{k_{1}},w_{k_{2}}
integrals, we obtain (
1.11
). This completes the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

12.1.
Technical lemmas required in this section

Lemma 12.4
.

Let
\Phi_{i}
be an even Schwartz function for each
i\leq n
. We have

\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{O^{-}(2N+2)}\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{\begin{subarray}{c}-N\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{n}\leq N\\
j_{1},\dots,j_{n}\neq 0\end{subarray}}\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}\Phi_{\ell}\bigg(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}\bigg)dX_{O^{-}(2N+2)}\\
=\lim_{N\to\infty}\int_{USp(2N)}\operatorname*{\sum...\sum}_{-N\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{n}\leq N}\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}\Phi_{\ell}\bigg(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}\bigg)dX_{USp(2N)}.

Proof.

By
[
28
, Theorem AD.2.2]
we find that

\lim_{N\to\infty}\ \int_{O^{-}(2N+2)}\ \sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{1\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{n}\leq N}\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}\Phi_{\ell}\bigg(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}\bigg)dX_{O^{-}(2N+2)}\\
=\lim_{N\to\infty}\ \int_{USp(2N)}\ \sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{1\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{n}\leq N}\penalty\ \prod_{\ell=1}^{n}\Phi_{\ell}\bigg(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}\bigg)dX_{USp(2N)}.

By (
2.12
) with

C_{\underline{G}}=\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots j_{\nu}\leq N}\prod_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\Phi_{G_{\ell}}\bigg(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}\bigg),\qquad R_{\underline{G}}=\sideset{}{{}^{\#}}{\sum}_{1\leq j_{1},\ldots j_{\nu}\leq N}\prod_{\ell=1}^{\nu}\Phi_{G_{\ell}}\bigg(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}\bigg)

for
\underline{G}=\{G_{1},\ldots,G_{\nu}\}\in\Pi_{n}
, we have
\displaystyle C_{\underline{O}}=\sum_{\underline{G}\in\Pi_{n}}R_{\underline{G}}
, in other words,

\lim_{N\to\infty}\ \int_{O^{-}(2N+2)}\ \sum_{1\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{n}\leq N}\prod_{\ell=1}^{n}\Phi_{\ell}\bigg(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}\bigg)dX_{O^{-}(2N+2)}\\
=\lim_{N\to\infty}\ \int_{USp(2N)}\sum_{1\leq j_{1},\dots,j_{n}\leq N}\penalty\ \prod_{\ell=1}^{n}\Phi_{\ell}\bigg(\frac{N\theta_{j_{\ell}}}{\pi}\bigg)dX_{USp(2N)}.

By symmetry, the lemma holds.
∎

Lemma 12.5
.

Let
\delta,U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
. Assume that
\Phi_{1}
and
\Phi_{2}
are even and their Fourier transforms are compactly supported. Then we have

\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\delta)}\frac{e^{-2\pi U_{1}w}}{w-z}\Phi_{1}(iw)dw=\begin{cases}\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t+U_{1})e^{2\pi tz}dt&\mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }\delta>\textup{Re}(z),\\
-\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t-U_{1})e^{-2\pi tz}dt&\mathrm{\penalty\ if\penalty\ }\delta<\textup{Re}(z),\end{cases}

and

\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{2})}\int_{(\delta_{1})}\frac{e^{-2\pi(U_{1}w_{1}+U_{2}w_{2})}}{(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}}\Phi_{1}(iw_{1})\Phi_{2}(iw_{2})dw_{1}dw_{2}=\int_{0}^{\infty}t\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t+U_{1})\widehat{\Phi}_{2}(t+U_{2})dt.

In particular, by letting
U_{1}=z=0

\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\delta_{1})}\frac{1}{w_{1}}\Phi_{1}(iw_{1})dw_{1}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t)dt=\frac{\Phi_{1}(0)}{2}.

Proof.

By Fourier inversion, we have that

(12.28)

e^{-2\pi Uw}\Phi(iw)=e^{-2\pi Uw}\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}(t)e^{-2\pi tw}dt=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}(t-U)e^{-2\pi tw}dt

for any real
U
. Thus, if
\delta>\textup{Re}(z)
,

\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\delta)}\frac{e^{-2\pi U_{1}w}}{w-z}\Phi_{1}(iw)dw=\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t_{1}-U_{1})\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\delta)}\frac{e^{-2\pi t_{1}w}}{w-z}dwdt_{1}\\
=\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t_{1}-U_{1})e^{-2\pi t_{1}z}dt_{1}=\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t+U_{1})e^{2\pi tz}dt.

For
\delta<\textup{Re}(z)
, the formula follows by the same arguments.

For the second expression, by (
12.28
), we obtain that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}

\displaystyle\int_{(\delta_{2})}\int_{(\delta_{1})}\frac{e^{-2\pi(U_{1}w_{1}+U_{2}w_{2})}}{(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}}\Phi_{1}(iw_{1})\Phi_{2}(iw_{2})dw_{1}dw_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t_{1}-U_{1})\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{2})}\int_{(\delta_{1})}\frac{e^{-2\pi t_{1}w_{1}}}{(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}}dw_{1}e^{-2\pi U_{2}w_{2}}\Phi_{2}(iw_{2})dw_{2}dt_{1}.

For
t_{1}>0
, we shift the
w_{1}
-contour far to the right and the
w_{1}
-integral is zero. For
t_{1}\leq 0
, we shift the
w_{1}
-contour far to the left and pick up a residue at
w_{1}=-w_{2}
. Hence, the above equals

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t_{1}-U_{1})\frac{1}{2\pi i}\int_{(\delta_{2})}(-2\pi t_{1})e^{2\pi t_{1}w_{2}}e^{-2\pi U_{2}w_{2}}\Phi_{2}(iw_{2})dw_{2}dt_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{-\infty}^{0}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t_{1}-U_{1})(-t_{1})\widehat{\Phi}_{2}(t_{1}-U_{2})dt_{1}=\int_{0}^{\infty}t\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t+U_{1})\widehat{\Phi}_{2}(t+U_{2})dt.

∎

Lemma 12.6
.

Let
\delta_{1},\delta_{2}>0
and
U_{1},U_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{1,2}:=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{1})}\int_{(\delta_{2})}e^{-2\pi U_{1}w_{1}-2\pi U_{2}w_{2}}\frac{(w_{1}-w_{2})^{2}}{w_{1}w_{2}(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}}\Phi_{1}(iw_{1})\Phi_{2}(iw_{2})dw_{2}dw_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t+U_{1})dt\int_{0}^{\infty}\widehat{\Phi}_{2}(t+U_{2})dt-4\int_{0}^{\infty}t\widehat{\Phi}_{1}(t+U_{1})\widehat{\Phi}_{2}(t+U_{2})dt.

Proof.

Since

\frac{(w_{1}-w_{2})^{2}}{w_{1}w_{2}(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}}=\frac{(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}-4w_{1}w_{2}}{w_{1}w_{2}(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}}=\frac{1}{w_{1}w_{2}}-\frac{4}{(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}},

we have

\displaystyle\mathscr{I}_{1,2}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{1})}\int_{(\delta_{2})}e^{-2\pi U_{1}w_{1}-2\pi U_{2}w_{2}}\frac{1}{w_{1}w_{2}}\Phi_{1}(iw_{1})\Phi_{2}(iw_{2})dw_{2}dw_{1}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{(2\pi i)^{2}}\int_{(\delta_{1})}\int_{(\delta_{2})}e^{-2\pi U_{1}w_{1}-2\pi U_{2}w_{2}}\frac{4}{(w_{1}+w_{2})^{2}}\Phi_{1}(iw_{1})\Phi_{2}(iw_{2})dw_{2}dw_{1}.

The lemma follows by applying Lemma
12.5
to the above.
∎
